<a id="italy-migration-guide"></a>

# Italy: migration guide

Sources checked up to 10 Nov 2026.

How to go to Italy on a Bangladeshi passport, visa by visa, step by step. A raised number such as 3 is an official source; N3 is a news, NGO, research or guide source, listed under "Needs an official source"; a year, like N3 (2022), marks a source older than 2025. "Need to know" marks a point no source has yet confirmed. "You'll need:" lists what to have before starting a procedure.

Tk is the Bangladeshi taka (1 lakh is Tk 100,000; 1 crore is 100 lakh); EUR is the euro, converted throughout at EUR 1 = about Tk 139.6, the Bangladesh Bank cross rate on 10 November 2026, and USD at USD 1 = Tk 124.42, Bangladesh Bank's rate the same day <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>. The Italian Embassy in Dhaka and VFS Global charge visa fees in cash, in taka, at their own rate of EUR 1 = Tk 140 <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>. BMET (Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) is the Bangladesh government agency that registers jobseekers on its OEP (Overseas Employment Platform) website and gives every worker emigration clearance before departure <sup>[3](#o3), [4](#o4)</sup>. The Decreto Flussi ("flows decree") is Italy's quota of foreign workers, set by the Prime Minister: every entry for employed, seasonal or self-employed work must fall within it, except a few routes outside the quota <sup>[5](#o5), [6](#o6), [7](#o7)</sup>. A nulla osta (Italian for "no objection") is the authorisation an employer in Italy gets to hire one named foreign worker; it is not permission to enter, and the Embassy decides the visa only after the employer confirms <sup>[N1](#n1), [8](#o8)</sup>. The SUI (Sportello Unico per l'Immigrazione) is the immigration desk at the Prefettura, the central government's office in each province; it runs the whole hiring procedure and issues the nulla osta <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. The permesso di soggiorno is the residence permit: a worker asks the Questura, the provincial police headquarters, for it within 8 working days of entering Italy, and the Questura also carries out expulsions <sup>[9](#o9), [5](#o5)</sup>. VFS Global takes every Italian visa application in Bangladesh for the Embassy and handles only the logistics, with no say in decisions <sup>[10](#o10), [11](#o11)</sup>.

<a id="background"></a>

## 1 Background

<a id="in-one-minute"></a>

### 1.1 In one minute

Italy takes Bangladeshi workers only through an employer in Italy who names them in a nulla osta under the yearly Decreto Flussi quota; about 10,000 Bangladeshis got Italian work visas in 2025, and about as many are expected in 2026 <sup>[N2](#n2), [5](#o5), [12](#o12)</sup>. A worker can't apply himself and can hardly find an Italian employer from Bangladesh, so a middleman sells the place: workers have paid EUR 7,000 to 20,000 (about Tk 9.8 to 27.9 lakh), against Tk 21,830 in official fees at VFS <sup>[N3](#n3), [N4](#n4), [N5](#n5), [2](#o2), [1](#o1)</sup>. Bangladesh's 2017 migration cost caps cover 14 countries and do not include Italy <sup>[13 (2019)](#o13)</sup>. Because of the high number of false and counterfeit documents, Italy froze every Bangladeshi nulla osta not yet turned into a visa in October 2024, and many of the old cases were rejected; new ones go through quickly, but only after the Questura and the labour inspectorate have checked them <sup>[14 (2024)](#o14), [15](#o15), [12](#o12), [16](#o16)</sup>. Many who paid and flew found no employer waiting, and some fell into irregular status <sup>[N4](#n4), [N6](#n6)</sup>. Once you hold a permit for work, losing or leaving the job does not cancel it and you may work for someone else, but the first permit depends on the employer signing your contract within 15 days of your arrival <sup>[8](#o8), [17](#o17)</sup>. Bangladeshis are also the largest nationality landing in Italy by sea (20,164 in 2025) and the largest group of asylum seekers, and nearly 9 in 10 of their asylum claims are rejected at first decision; Italy and the EU list Bangladesh as a safe country of origin, so a claim goes into the accelerated procedure and can be rejected as manifestly unfounded <sup>[18](#o18), [N7](#n7), [19](#o19), [20](#o20), [N8](#n8)</sup>.

<a id="is-it-open-now-and-who-can-go"></a>

### 1.2 Is it open now, and who can go?

Open as of 10 November 2026, through an employer's nulla osta under the Decreto Flussi <sup>[12](#o12), [5](#o5)</sup>. For the 2027 round, employers pre-fill applications until 7 December 2026 (and may finish saved forms until 13 December), and the click day for non-seasonal work is 16 February 2027 (["Work visa: Decreto Flussi worker (non-seasonal)"](#work-visa-decreto-flussi-worker-non-seasonal), [Step 3: The employer gets your nulla osta](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-nulla-osta)) <sup>[6](#o6), [21](#o21)</sup>. New nulla osta are processed in a relatively short time with very few refusals; many of the old ones frozen in October 2024 were rejected <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>. A Bangladeshi worker's nulla osta is never issued by silence: the Questura must approve it, and the INL (Ispettorato Nazionale del Lavoro, the National Labour Inspectorate) first checks the employer <sup>[16](#o16)</sup>.

<a id="status"></a>

#### 1.2.1 Status

- **11 October 2024:** Decree-Law 145/2024 suspended every nulla osta already issued to Bangladeshi, Pakistani and Sri Lankan workers who had no visa yet, because of the high number of false documents, and made new ones depend on the INL's prior check, with no automatic issue after 60 days <sup>[22 (2024)](#o22), [14 (2024)](#o14), [15](#o15)</sup>.
- **16-20 October 2024:** the Embassy said it would issue a work visa only after the SUI confirmed a suspended nulla osta, and from 20 October returned the passports of everyone waiting; a suspended nulla osta does not expire while it waits <sup>[14 (2024)](#o14), [22 (2024)](#o22)</sup>.
- **27 May 2025:** over 7,000 suspended cases had ended in a visa or a rejection, and over 70% of entries on the Embassy's work-visa webform carried fake or invalid nulla osta <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>.
- **August 2025:** about 8,000 of nearly 40,000 pending cases had been resolved; nulla osta issued after 22 October 2024 were not suspended <sup>[N9](#n9)</sup>. In July 2025 nearly 60,000 work visa applications were reported stuck at the Embassy; Italy's Ambassador later said 2025 applications were cleared quickly with no backlog, while those of 2023 and 2024 went back to the Questure for review with no time limit <sup>[N10](#n10), [N4](#n4)</sup>.
- **September 2025:** the SUIs had confirmed 944 nulla osta for Bangladeshis who had not yet applied for a visa, and the Embassy called them to walk-in days at the VFS centres in Dhaka, Chattogram and Sylhet <sup>[23](#o23)</sup>.
- **8 December 2025:** holders of a nulla osta issued or reconfirmed in 2025 register on VFS's portal and get an appointment within a few days; holders of a 2023-24 nulla osta who have already applied for a visa wait until the immigration office in Italy finishes its review (["Work visa: Decreto Flussi worker (non-seasonal)"](#work-visa-decreto-flussi-worker-non-seasonal), [Step 5: The visa at the Italian Embassy in Dhaka, and checking it is real](#step-5-the-visa-at-the-italian-embassy-in-dhaka-and-checking-it-is-real)) <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>. By 10 December 2025, 62,965 of the 329,001 applications filed for Bangladeshis in the 2024 round had ended negatively (about 20,000 archived and 22,000 withdrawn), about half of all negative outcomes that year, and 23,141 applications of that round for Bangladesh, Pakistan, Sri Lanka and Morocco were still suspended, 16,272 of them at the consulates <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do the Questura approval and the INL check still apply to Bangladeshi nulla osta in the 2027 round (the joint circular of 1 October 2026 does not mention them, and Decree-Law 145/2024's Bangladesh rule was not extended past 2025), and has the Foreign Ministry issued its decree naming high-risk countries?
> - How many of the 38,039 applications suspended in Dhaka in October 2024 ended in a visa?

<a id="the-channels"></a>

#### 1.2.2 The channels

- **Decreto Flussi, non-seasonal work (form B2020):** the employer files for a named worker on click day, the SUI issues the nulla osta, and the worker applies at VFS (see Work visa: Decreto Flussi worker (non-seasonal)) <sup>[21](#o21), [8](#o8), [10](#o10)</sup>. Italy admits 76,200 non-seasonal workers in 2027, 14,000 of them domestic and care workers <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>. Over 2026-2028 Italy plans 497,550 entries in all (164,850, 165,850 and 166,850 a year), and from 2027 Kenya joins the countries of origin after signing a migration and mobility memorandum with Italy on 2 July 2026 <sup>[N1](#n1), [6](#o6)</sup>. Bangladesh shares a reserved quota of 25,000 non-seasonal places a year in 2026-2028 with the other countries that have cooperation agreements with Italy, and such reserved quotas go to countries that cooperate on readmitting their returned nationals <sup>[25](#o25), [6](#o6), [5](#o5)</sup>. The quota usually fills in the first ten minutes of click day <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>.
- **Seasonal farm and tourism work:** 89,000 places in 2027, for at most nine months in twelve; many in Bangladesh call it the কৃষি ভিসা (krishi visa, agriculture visa) (see [Work visa: seasonal worker](#work-visa-seasonal-worker)) <sup>[6](#o6), [26](#o26), [N3](#n3)</sup>.
- **Domestic and care work:** the 14,000 places for jobs with a household, plus up to 10,000 out-of-quota carers a year (see [Work visa: domestic and care worker](#work-visa-domestic-and-care-worker)) <sup>[6](#o6), [16](#o16), [27](#o27)</sup>.
- **Article 23 of the immigration law: training first, outside the quota:** a worker who completes a pre-departure course certified by Italy's Ministry of Labour (basic Italian, civic education, job skills) enters outside the quota; applications run all year with no click day, and the course can be done in Bangladesh <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>. The first projects in Bangladesh were set up by Italian private firms: Frontiere Skills, whose TASTE project trains restaurant and hotel workers at Hotel Regency in Dhaka, and Format, which trains textile workers; about 300 students were enrolled in November 2026 <sup>[28](#o28), [N1](#n1)</sup>. Format says its training is free <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>. Several hundred Bangladeshis a year now go through such training <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>. Across all countries, 401 people trained abroad entered Italy this way in 2025, up from 133 in 2024 <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>. Separately, the EU-funded Talent Partnership, run by BMET with the ILO (International Labour Organization), had certified 698 trainees by July 2026, mainly for Italy, and many were waiting for employer interviews <sup>[N12](#n12), [N13](#n13)</sup>.
- **Highly skilled (EU Blue Card, Article 27, nulla osta code "L/N"):** outside the quota and only for highly qualified jobs; very few Bangladeshi applicants have succeeded (see Work visa: highly skilled and out-of-quota (EU Blue Card, Article 27)) <sup>[29](#o29)</sup>.
- **Self-employment:** 650 places a year for all countries together (see [Self-employment visa](#self-employment-visa)) <sup>[6](#o6)</sup>.
- **Family reunification:** about 12,000 to 13,000 family visas a year, and family members may work once they arrive (see [Family reunification](#family-reunification)) <sup>[30](#o30)</sup>.
- **Not a work route:** a Schengen visit visa (the short-stay visa for Italy and the other Schengen countries), which allows no work and which the Italian consulate in Dhaka refused to 60% of applicants in 2025 (see Visit visa (Schengen)) <sup>[31](#o31), [32](#o32)</sup>; and the boat from Libya (see [Irregular routes and undocumented workers](#irregular-routes-and-undocumented-workers)) <sup>[18](#o18)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many of the 25,000 reserved non-seasonal places go to Bangladeshis in 2026 and 2027?
> - How does a worker in Bangladesh get a place on an Article 23 course (Frontiere Skills, Format) or the Talent Partnership, and what does he pay at each stage?

<a id="who-qualifies"></a>

#### 1.2.3 Who qualifies

Anyone an employer in Italy names: the employer picks the worker at his discretion, and there is no public list of workers and no list of agencies an employer must use <sup>[N2](#n2), [N4](#n4)</sup>. The Article 23 route needs the certified course first, and the Blue Card a highly qualified job <sup>[N1](#n1), [33](#o33)</sup>. Few women go: 76.7% of Bangladeshis in Italy are men, and BMET cleared 261 women for Italy from 1 January to 9 November 2026 <sup>[34](#o34), [35](#o35)</sup>. Italian is the only language at work <sup>[N14](#n14)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do employers or the Embassy test a Decreto Flussi worker's Italian or job skills before the visa?

<a id="size"></a>

#### 1.2.4 Size

- **Workers cleared by BMET:** 16,846 in 2023, 1,162 in 2024, 9,365 in 2025 and 8,614 from 1 January to 9 November 2026, when Italy was BMET's 8th-largest destination <sup>[36](#o36), [37](#o37), [38](#o38), [39](#o39), [40](#o40)</sup>.
- **Month by month:** 4,043 in July 2023, 59 in December 2023, 11 in September 2024, 1,644 in August 2025 and 690 in October 2026 <sup>[41](#o41), [42](#o42), [43](#o43), [44](#o44)</sup>.
- **Job titles on OEP:** the largest 2026 titles are the vague "A Casual Worker" (2,842) and "General Labour" (2,427) <sup>[45](#o45)</sup>.
- **Work visas:** about 10,000 in 2025, and about as many expected in 2026 <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>.
- **Applications against visas:** Bangladeshis filed almost 58% of all Decreto Flussi applications in 2023 (352,562) and 45.8% in 2024 (328,877), far ahead of Morocco (15.8%) and India (14.2%) <sup>[N5](#n5)</sup>. In 2024 Sri Lanka filed 6.6% of applications, Pakistan 4.8% and Egypt 4.1%, and Bangladeshis also had the most negative outcomes: 39,397, or 52.4% of the total <sup>[N5](#n5)</sup>. By 12 December 2024, of 24,151 visas issued for the 2024 quota, 35 had gone to Bangladeshis and 1,866 to Sri Lankans; for the 2023 quota, Bangladeshis got 3,409 <sup>[N5](#n5)</sup>. By December 2025 the 2024 round had given Bangladeshis 19,503 nulla osta, 2,313 visas and 1,262 permit requests, with 1,058 still waiting to travel; in the 2025 round applications for Bangladeshis fell to 35,932, with 2,407 nulla osta, 1,874 visas and 1,038 permit requests <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>. By permits requested in the 2025 round, Bangladesh came sixth, after India, Egypt, Tunisia, Morocco and Albania <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>.
- **Living in Italy:** 195,523 Bangladeshis held permits at the end of 2024, the fifth-largest non-EU community; Rome has 37,905 of them (19.4%), followed by Lombardy, Veneto, Campania and Sicily <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>. The community grew 16.9% in 2024, and after the United Kingdom Italy is the main European destination for Bangladeshi migrants <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>. Of the 28,045 new permits Bangladeshis got in 2024, two-thirds came from asylum and protection claims and only just over one in ten from work <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>.
- **By boat:** 20,164 Bangladeshis landed by sea in 2025 and 6,160 from 1 January to 6 November 2026, more than any other nationality <sup>[18](#o18), [46](#o46)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does "A Casual Worker" on OEP mean a seasonal nulla osta, and what does the title "Agent" (616 Italy clearances in 2025) mean <sup>[47](#o47)</sup>?

<a id="what-closed-it-before"></a>

#### 1.2.5 What closed it before

- **2012-13:** Italy took away Bangladesh's quota after Bangladeshi seasonal farm workers did not go home after the season, and formal labour migration stopped in 2013 <sup>[N15 (2023)](#n15), [N16](#n16)</sup>.
- **2020-21:** Italy put Bangladesh back on the Decreto Flussi list on 12 October 2020, and workers began going again in 2021, 653 that year <sup>[N15 (2023)](#n15), [N16](#n16), [N14](#n14)</sup>.
- **4 June 2024:** Prime Minister Giorgia Meloni took a complaint to the national anti-mafia prosecutor, Giovanni Melillo, saying most Decreto Flussi entrants came from Bangladesh, where diplomats spoke of jobs being bought and sold, and that in Campania, with 157,000 applications, under 3% of those given a seasonal visa signed a contract <sup>[N17 (2024)](#n17), [N18 (2024)](#n18)</sup>.
- **October 2024:** the freeze (see [Status](#status)) suspended 34,959 of 38,039 applications in Dhaka (92%), and BMET cleared only 1,162 workers for Italy that year <sup>[N5](#n5), [37](#o37)</sup>. Applicants had formed human chains at the Embassy on 27 March and 22 April 2024, demanding visas or their passports back, when the Embassy and VFS were holding at least 100,000 applicants' passports <sup>[N19 (2024)](#n19), [N20 (2024)](#n20)</sup>. Visa processing has sometimes taken over a year, and in 2025 affected applicants protested in Dhaka's Kakrail area <sup>[N16](#n16), [N14](#n14)</sup>.
- **6 May 2025:** Bangladesh and Italy signed a Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) on Migration and Mobility in Dhaka, covering legal migration and the return of people with no right to stay <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>.

<a id="how-a-job-reaches-you-and-who-takes-a-cut"></a>

### 1.3 How a job reaches you, and who takes a cut

A legal job reaches a Bangladeshi worker through a person, not a list: an employer in Italy files for a worker he names, and the name usually comes through a Bangladeshi already in Italy or a broker <sup>[N2](#n2), [N15 (2023)](#n15), [N19 (2024)](#n19)</sup>. The choke point is the nulla osta: only the employer can request it, and a worker can't apply himself and can hardly find an employer from Bangladesh, so a middleman sells the place <sup>[N3](#n3), [8](#o8)</sup>. The Bangladesh Embassy's labour secretary in Rome has said a group exploits a process that needs "a third party as personal reference" <sup>[N15 (2023)](#n15)</sup>.

<a id="the-quota-and-the-click-day"></a>

#### 1.3.1 The quota and the click day

The government sets the quota; employers pre-fill their applications online, then race to send them on click day, which are handled mostly in order of arrival <sup>[49](#o49), [50](#o50)</sup>. Applications ran at about five times the places: 609,000 for 132,000 in 2023 and 718,000 for 147,000 in 2024 <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>. Few end in a job: of 127,707 places given out for 2023, only 16,188 (about 13%) led to a signed contract and a permit request <sup>[N5](#n5)</sup>. By December 2025 the 2024 round had produced 24,858 permit requests for 146,850 places (16.9%), and the 2025 round 14,349 for 181,450 (7.9%) <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>. Since October 2025 a private employer may file at most three requests a year; more must go through an employer organisation or labour consultant, who must keep the number in line with the firm's turnover and staff (["Work visa: Decreto Flussi worker (non-seasonal)"](#work-visa-decreto-flussi-worker-non-seasonal), [Step 2: The job offer and contract](#step-2-the-job-offer-and-contract)) <sup>[49](#o49), [8](#o8)</sup>.

<a id="who-sells-the-nulla-osta"></a>

#### 1.3.2 Who sells the nulla osta

- **Firms filing for jobs that don't exist:** in Campania a ring filed about 2,000 fictitious requests, and each migrant paid EUR 7,000 to 8,000 (about Tk 9.8 to 11.2 lakh) <sup>[N21](#n21), [1](#o1)</sup>. In Grosseto in September 2025 a Bangladeshi was arrested as the alleged head of a network that brought in nearly 200 Bangladeshis for fake employers, many of whom did not know their names were used, at EUR 2,000 to 4,000 (Tk 2.8 to 5.6 lakh) an application <sup>[N22](#n22), [1](#o1)</sup>. In June 2026, 30 people were arrested over false applications filed through a CAF (tax assistance centre) in Taranto, mostly for Pakistanis, Bangladeshis and Indians, who paid up to EUR 6,500 (about Tk 9.1 lakh) <sup>[N23](#n23), [1](#o1)</sup>. In a Lecce-Taranto investigation, businesses filed "simulated" hiring requests only to obtain nulla osta, and one intercepted call called it "business di bangladesi" <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>.
- **Consultants and patronati:** in the Campania case a lawyer filed click-day applications through his staff for at least EUR 5,000 (about Tk 7 lakh) a file, and staff of patronati (union-linked welfare advice offices) filed requests for a fee <sup>[N21](#n21), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Officials:** officials of the Prefettura and the Labour Inspectorate in Naples and Salerno allegedly took EUR 500 to 800 (about Tk 70,000 to 1.12 lakh) a file <sup>[N21](#n21), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Bangladeshi brokers:** a broker in Bangladesh with "a relative in Italy who handles legal entries", or a Bangladeshi with an agency in Italy who says he has firms ready to hire; workers call them দালাল (dalal) <sup>[N5](#n5), [N24](#n24)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - In 2026, who in Italy sells a nulla osta to a Bangladeshi broker (the employer, a consultant, a Bangladeshi-run agency), and for how much?
> - What does the employer himself get for each nulla osta he files?

<a id="the-embassy-queue"></a>

#### 1.3.3 The embassy queue

Visas at the Italian Embassy in Dhaka were themselves sold <sup>[N25](#n25), [N26](#n26)</sup>. On 19 February 2025 two Italian Foreign Ministry employees, Nicola Muscatello and Roberto Albergo, were put under house arrest for corruption, and two Bangladeshis, Nazrul Islam, who lives in Rome, and Hossain Md Baleyet Patwari, were jailed, in an investigation by the Guardia di Finanza (Italy's financial police) <sup>[N25](#n25), [N26](#n26)</sup>. Italy's Foreign Ministry confirmed that the Rome prosecutor's custody order covered five people in all, and said criminal networks in Italy profit from Decreto Flussi visas and fake tourist visas <sup>[51](#o51)</sup>. Prosecutors say the two Bangladeshis charged compatriots a fixed sum plus a share of their future earnings in Italy, and that the officials took hotel stays, phones, watches and flights <sup>[N25](#n25)</sup>. The ring allegedly charged EUR 15,000 (about Tk 20.9 lakh) for a work visa and EUR 7,000 for a tourist visa, and Nazrul Islam, linked to the Awami League's Italian branch, had brought nearly 1,500 Bangladeshis the year before <sup>[N26](#n26), [1](#o1)</sup>. Investigators also say embassy staff paid real Italian firms to join the scheme <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has the Rome court ruled on the Muscatello, Albergo and Nazrul Islam case, and were visas issued through the ring revoked?

<a id="the-broker-and-the-village"></a>

#### 1.3.4 The broker and the village

Many irregular migrants come from Shariatpur and Madaripur, the Italian Ambassador says, and on the legal route a Madaripur man paid a broker EUR 16,000 (about Tk 22.3 lakh) for promised contract work <sup>[52](#o52), [N24](#n24), [1](#o1)</sup>. The middleman can be a relative: a Narsingdi man went because a cousin in Italy offered to help him find work, paid a broker EUR 16,000 (about Tk 22.3 lakh), and no employer ever contacted him <sup>[N24](#n24), [1](#o1)</sup>. A Naples farm owner applied for workers through a Bangladeshi already working on his farm <sup>[N19 (2024)](#n19)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does a village dalal take on an Italy deal, and is he paid by the worker, the main broker or both?

<a id="what-you-pay-and-what-you-earn-and-keep"></a>

### 1.4 What you pay, and what you earn and keep

The official costs are small: Tk 21,830 at VFS, a EUR 16 revenue stamp, the flight and BMET's fees <sup>[2](#o2), [N15 (2023)](#n15)</sup>. Workers pay brokers EUR 7,000 to 20,000 (about Tk 9.8 to 27.9 lakh), and many arrive to no job <sup>[N4](#n4), [N5](#n5), [1](#o1)</sup>.

<a id="the-cap"></a>

#### 1.4.1 The cap

Bangladesh sets no legal maximum for Italy: the Ministry's 2017 migration-cost caps cover 14 countries, and Italy is not one of them <sup>[13 (2019)](#o13)</sup>. Italy sets no migration cost for the worker either <sup>[N15 (2023)](#n15)</sup>. The official costs are:

- visa fee: EUR 116 (Tk 16,240) <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>;
- VFS service charge: EUR 38 (Tk 5,320), VAT included <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>;
- bank draft charge: Tk 270 <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>;
- revenue stamp: EUR 16 (about Tk 2,200) <sup>[N15 (2023)](#n15), [1](#o1)</sup>;
- the flight to Italy, paid by the worker <sup>[N15 (2023)](#n15)</sup>;
- BMET's fees: a Tk 5,500 welfare fee, Tk 1,000 insurance and a Tk 250 clearance fee (["Work visa: Decreto Flussi worker (non-seasonal)"](#work-visa-decreto-flussi-worker-non-seasonal), [Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance and the flight](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight)) <sup>[53](#o53), [54](#o54), [4](#o4)</sup>.

The contract of stay commits the employer to pay your journey home <sup>[55](#o55)</sup>. Workers have complained that the whole cost should be no more than Tk 2 to 2.5 lakh <sup>[N15 (2023)](#n15)</sup>.

<a id="what-people-really-pay"></a>

#### 1.4.2 What people really pay

- **USD 10,000 to 15,000 (about Tk 12.4 to 18.7 lakh):** what brokers charge, the Embassy's November 2026 seminar heard <sup>[N1](#n1), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **EUR 15,000 (about Tk 20.9 lakh):** what Prime Minister Meloni said migrants paid for click days, and the work-visa price of the 2025 embassy bribery ring <sup>[N21](#n21), [N26](#n26), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **EUR 7,000 up front and as much again on the visa (about Tk 19.5 lakh in all):** a Gazipur man in 2025 <sup>[N4](#n4), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **EUR 16,000 (about Tk 22.3 lakh):** two men from Madaripur and Narsingdi <sup>[N24](#n24), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **EUR 18,000 and EUR 20,000 (about Tk 25.1 and 27.9 lakh):** two workers who spoke to Ero Straniero, a campaign of Italian NGOs <sup>[N5](#n5), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Tk 6 to 10 lakh:** the middleman's take, with workers reporting up to Tk 15 lakh in all; a Naogaon man paid Tk 13.5 lakh for his work permit (nulla osta) in May 2023 and was still waiting for his visa a year later <sup>[N3](#n3), [N15 (2023)](#n15), [N20 (2024)](#n20)</sup>.

<a id="how-it-is-paid"></a>

#### 1.4.3 How it is paid

In instalments: a first payment starts the paperwork and the rest is due before or when the visa comes; one worker paid EUR 8,000 (about Tk 11.2 lakh) up front and the rest before the visa <sup>[N5](#n5), [N4](#n4), [1](#o1)</sup>. The passport is held as security: his broker kept it until the last instalment <sup>[N5](#n5)</sup>. The 2025 bribery ring took part of its fee as a share of future wages in Italy <sup>[N25](#n25)</sup>. The Embassy tells anyone using an agency to take a receipt for every payment and to know every document filed in their name, and says neither it nor VFS will ever hold a passport against the applicant's will <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - In a 2026 Italy deal, how much is paid at the start, how much when the nulla osta comes, and how much when the visa is stamped?

<a id="how-they-borrow"></a>

#### 1.4.4 How they borrow

Families sell land, pool savings and take bank loans: the Madaripur man took loans from two banks, the Narsingdi man raised EUR 16,000 from relatives, loans and the sale of family land, and in the Grosseto case some families mortgaged land <sup>[N24](#n24), [N22](#n22)</sup>. Loans are easier to get when the money is said to be for Italy <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>. One worker said his bank debt grew daily with interest <sup>[N5](#n5)</sup>. The PKB (Probashi Kallyan Bank, the government bank for migrants) lends at most Tk 3 lakh (["Work visa: Decreto Flussi worker (non-seasonal)"](#work-visa-decreto-flussi-worker-non-seasonal), [Step 1: Find a real job](#step-1-find-a-real-job)) <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What monthly rate does a village moneylender charge on an Italy loan in 2026?
> - Does PKB lend against an Italian nulla osta, or only once the visa is stamped?

<a id="what-the-job-pays"></a>

#### 1.4.5 What the job pays

32% of employed Bangladeshis in Italy work in hotels and restaurants and 25.3% in industry <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>. Minimum pay is set by the CCNL (contratto collettivo nazionale di lavoro, the national collective contract) for each sector and is quoted gross, before tax and contributions <sup>[N28](#n28), [N29](#n29), [N30](#n30)</sup>. The 2026 minimums include:

- **Farm work:** EUR 1,042.43 a month (about Tk 1.46 lakh), the national floor for a common worker <sup>[N28](#n28), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Construction:** EUR 9.48 an hour (about Tk 1,320) for a common labourer, plus provincial extras <sup>[N29](#n29), [N31](#n31), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Hotels:** EUR 1,412.60 a month (about Tk 1.97 lakh) at the lowest level, paid 14 times a year <sup>[N30](#n30), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Cleaning:** EUR 1,413.50 a month (about Tk 1.97 lakh) full-time <sup>[N32](#n32), [1](#o1)</sup>.

Full tables are under Your rights at work. In practice, unskilled or seasonal workers earn at most Tk 1 to 1.5 lakh a month, and skilled workers EUR 1,500 to 2,500 (about Tk 2.1 to 3.5 lakh) <sup>[N3](#n3), [1](#o1)</sup>. Wages must be paid through a bank or post office, never in cash, and an employer who pays cash is fined EUR 1,000 to 5,000 <sup>[57 (2018)](#o57)</sup>. Income tax and the end of tax deductions for family in Bangladesh: see [Sending money home](#sending-money-home).

<a id="what-goes-unpaid"></a>

#### 1.4.6 What goes unpaid

- **Pay far below the contract:** one worker taken from the airport by his broker worked 16 to 17 hours a day for EUR 400 to 500 a month (about Tk 56,000 to 70,000), and paid EUR 140 (about Tk 19,500) of it back to the middleman as rent <sup>[N5](#n5), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Payslips that lie:** among Fincantieri shipyard subcontractors, whose workers included Bangladeshis, payslips carried items never paid, and 383 workers took under EUR 7 (about Tk 980) an hour because they needed the job to renew their permit (see [Your rights at work](#your-rights-at-work)) <sup>[N33 (2023)](#n33), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Gangmaster pay on farms:** near Naples in 2025 prosecutors found farm workers paid about EUR 2.70 an hour for 10 to 14-hour days (see [Caporalato: illegal gangmasters](#caporalato-illegal-gangmasters)) <sup>[58](#o58)</sup>.

How to claim: see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid).

<a id="money-sent-home"></a>

#### 1.4.7 Money sent home

Italy is one of Bangladesh's largest sources of remittances: USD 2,051.5 million (about Tk 25,500 crore) in July 2025 to June 2026, up from USD 810.9 million five years earlier, and USD 602.24 million in July to September 2026, sixth among all countries <sup>[59](#o59), [60](#o60), [1](#o1)</sup>. Bangladesh received 19.4% of all money-transfer flows leaving Italy in the year to June 2026, more than any other country, and the province of Rome alone sent EUR 364.9 million (about Tk 5,100 crore) in 2025 <sup>[61](#o61), [62](#o62), [1](#o1)</sup>. The provinces of Milan (EUR 159.9 million), Naples (EUR 138.5 million) and Venice (EUR 107.9 million) followed in 2025; recorded transfers from Italy to Bangladesh were about EUR 1,686 million that year, but fell to EUR 795.8 million in the first half of 2026 from EUR 834.9 million a year earlier <sup>[62](#o62)</sup>. Sending from Italy cost 1.27% of the amount on average in April to June 2026 (how to send: see [Sending money home](#sending-money-home)) <sup>[63](#o63)</sup>. Only 77% of Bangladeshi adults in Italy have a bank account <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>. Hundi (money passed outside the banks through private agents) is illegal in Bangladesh, and by June 2026 the BFIU (Bangladesh Financial Intelligence Unit) had frozen or suspended about 55,000 mobile-money accounts linked to digital hundi and online gambling <sup>[N34 (2024)](#n34), [N35](#n35)</sup>. Workers without papers have no formal channel, which keeps hundi alive <sup>[N34 (2024)](#n34)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What taka rate per euro do hundi agents in Rome (Torpignattara) and Milan offer in November 2026, against the bank rate of about Tk 139.6?

<a id="how-long-the-debt-takes"></a>

#### 1.4.8 How long the debt takes

A seasonal worker who paid Tk 6 lakh can't save it back in one nine-month season <sup>[N3](#n3)</sup>. A EUR 15,000 deal equals more than 14 months of the farm minimum before tax <sup>[N21](#n21), [N28](#n28)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How long does a non-seasonal worker who paid EUR 15,000 and has a real job take to clear the debt, after rent and food?

<a id="what-ties-you-to-the-employer"></a>

### 1.5 What ties you to the employer

The nulla osta ties you to one employer until you arrive and sign your contract; after that the permit for work is looser: losing or leaving the job does not cancel it, and it lets you do other legal work <sup>[8](#o8), [17](#o17)</sup>.

- **To enter:** only the employer, or its employer organisation or labour consultant, can file the nulla osta naming you, and after the Embassy's checks it must confirm within 15 days or the nulla osta is revoked (["Work visa: Decreto Flussi worker (non-seasonal)"](#work-visa-decreto-flussi-worker-non-seasonal), [Step 3: The employer gets your nulla osta](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-nulla-osta); "Work visa: Decreto Flussi worker (non-seasonal)", [Step 5: The visa at the Italian Embassy in Dhaka, and checking it is real](#step-5-the-visa-at-the-italian-embassy-in-dhaka-and-checking-it-is-real)) <sup>[N2](#n2), [8](#o8), [6](#o6)</sup>.
- **To get the first permit:** the employer must sign the contratto di soggiorno (contract of stay) with you and send it online to the SUI within 15 days of your entry, or the nulla osta is revoked unless the delay was not your fault; until then you work only on the nulla osta (["Work visa: Decreto Flussi worker (non-seasonal)"](#work-visa-decreto-flussi-worker-non-seasonal), [Step 7: After arrival](#step-7-after-arrival)) <sup>[6](#o6), [8](#o8)</sup>. In practice workers have waited up to a year for a Prefettura appointment while employers disappeared, and one worker could work only for the employer that had filed for him, with no permit or health care, because it was behind on its social security payments <sup>[N6](#n6), [N5](#n5)</sup>. In August 2026 Bangladesh told Italy that several hundred workers who entered in 2022-23 could not change employer or extend their permits because their sponsors could not be reached <sup>[N36](#n36)</sup>. If the employer never signs, a job-seeking permit needs the written authorisation of the head of the SUI, and Questure issued only 666 job-seeking permits in all in 2025 (see [Changing employer](#changing-employer)) <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>.
- **To renew:** the permit lasts as long as the contract, up to 1 year for a fixed-term contract and 2 years for a permanent one, and each renewal up to 3 years; you ask the Questura of the province where you live at least 90 days before it expires (see [Your residence permit](#your-residence-permit)) <sup>[9](#o9)</sup>. Workers have accepted under EUR 7 an hour because they needed the job to renew <sup>[N33 (2023)](#n33)</sup>.
- **To quit:** resign only online on the Ministry of Labour form, since a paper letter has no effect; resigning does not cancel the permit, but it loses NASpI (unemployment pay) unless you resign for just cause (see [Changing employer or leaving](#changing-employer-or-leaving)) <sup>[64](#o64), [8](#o8), [65](#o65)</sup>.
- **To change employer:** a permit for employment can be used for other legal work <sup>[17](#o17)</sup>. Blue Card holders and out-of-quota carers need the labour office's approval to change employer in their first 12 months, and seasonal workers change through SIISL, the government job-matching platform (see [Changing employer](#changing-employer)) <sup>[66](#o66), [27](#o27), [26](#o26)</sup>.
- **To leave:** the contract of stay commits the employer to pay your journey home, and a seasonal worker must go home when the nine months end (see [Leaving at the end of the job](#leaving-at-the-end-of-the-job)) <sup>[55](#o55), [26](#o26)</sup>.
- **Which contract covers you:** a foreign worker with a valid permit has the same rights at work as an Italian, minimum pay follows the CCNL for the sector, and overtime is paid at the premiums it sets <sup>[N29](#n29), [N30](#n30), [67](#o67)</sup>; bars and restaurants have their own contract (FIPE), separate from hotels, and domestic workers theirs (see [Your rights at work](#your-rights-at-work)) <sup>[68](#o68), [67](#o67), [N30](#n30), [N37](#n37)</sup>.
- **If you lose the job:** register as available for work and you may stay for the rest of the permit and at least one year (not on a seasonal permit); after that a new permit depends on your income (see [If you lose your job](#if-you-lose-your-job)) <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.
- **If the permit expires:** while a renewal is pending you may stay and work with the post-office receipt, but if it expired over 60 days ago with no renewal requested, the Prefect (the government's representative in the province) orders expulsion, and staying without a permit is fined EUR 5,000 to 10,000 (about Tk 7 to 14 lakh) <sup>[49](#o49), [69](#o69), [70](#o70), [1](#o1)</sup>. People who lost a regular job, and with it their permit, have been detained and sent to Italy's detention centre in Albania (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the Questura accept a different employer at the first renewal?

<a id="what-goes-wrong-most-and-what-people-wrongly-believe"></a>

### 1.6 What goes wrong most, and what people wrongly believe

A common failure is paying for a nulla osta and arriving to no job, no contract and no permit <sup>[N2](#n2), [N4](#n4), [N5](#n5)</sup>. A Naples migrant network counts more than 1,200 people caught in one Italy-Bangladesh visa-fraud scheme over four years, and its help desk sees at least 100 Bangladeshis with the same problem every Thursday <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>. Bangladeshis who came on the Decreto Flussi and found no employer, or a scam, have formed a committee, Tikase <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>. Ital-Bangla, a Bangladeshi migrants' association, estimates that about 80% of those who entered on work visas in 2020-2023 became undocumented <sup>[N16](#n16)</sup>. Before departure the loss is money paid for a nulla osta that is fake or rejected (see [Status](#status)) <sup>[11](#o11), [12](#o12)</sup>.

<a id="what-people-believe-and-the-facts"></a>

#### 1.6.1 What people believe, and the facts

- **"A nulla osta means I can go."** It only authorises the employer to hire you: the Embassy decides the visa, and only after the employer confirms <sup>[N1](#n1), [8](#o8), [6](#o6)</sup>. Many 2023-24 nulla osta were frozen and then rejected (see [Status](#status)) <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>.
- **"With the visa, a job is waiting."** Many found no employer: one man fell into irregular status because the company that was to hire him did not exist <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>. Only about 13% of the 2023 quota, 16.9% of the 2024 quota and 7.9% of the 2025 quota had ended in a contract and a permit request at the last count (see [The quota and the click day](#the-quota-and-the-click-day)) <sup>[N5](#n5), [N11](#n11)</sup>.
- **"I came legally, so Italy will treat me as a victim."** A Naples social worker says the state treats such workers as fraudsters, and many try special protection instead, a two-year permit that can no longer be turned into a work permit (see [Asylum](#asylum)) <sup>[N24](#n24), [N39](#n39)</sup>.
- **"Someone at the Embassy can speed it up."** Officials and middlemen were arrested over exactly this offer in 2025 (see [The embassy queue](#the-embassy-queue)), and the Embassy says paying intermediaries risks cancellation of the visa and legal action <sup>[N25](#n25), [N40](#n40)</sup>.
- **"An L/N 'carta blu' nulla osta is a sure visa."** Brokers sell it, but it is only for highly qualified jobs, holding one does not guarantee a visa, and very few Bangladeshi applicants have got one <sup>[29](#o29)</sup>.
- **"Parents can no longer be brought to Italy."** A Bangla news site reported this in November 2025, but the law in force and the Embassy's July 2026 checklist still allow a dependent parent with no other children in Bangladesh, or a parent over 65 whose other children can't support them for documented serious health reasons (see [Family reunification](#family-reunification)) <sup>[N41](#n41), [71](#o71), [72](#o72)</sup>.
- **"Political asylum is easy in Italy."** Italy and the EU list Bangladesh as a safe country of origin, and Italy rejected 88.66% of Bangladeshi claims at first decision in 2025 (see [Asylum](#asylum)) <sup>[19](#o19), [20](#o20), [N7](#n7)</sup>.
- **"Go on a tourist visa and get papers there."** A Schengen visa doesn't allow work, study or applying to stay, and Bangladesh's Ministry warns never to go on a visit visa on a broker's promise (see [Changing visa type](#changing-visa-type)) <sup>[31](#o31), [73](#o73)</sup>.
- **"Italy will regularise us again."** Past regularisations are a main reason people go, and in the 2020 regularisation Bangladeshis' applications were for domestic and care work (16,102) and for farming, livestock and fishing (2,222) <sup>[N27](#n27), [74 (2020)](#o74)</sup>.

<a id="irregular-routes-and-where-an-irregular-stay-ends"></a>

#### 1.6.2 Irregular routes, and where an irregular stay ends

- **By boat from Libya: it ends at a hotspot, sometimes after ransom, and usually in a refused asylum claim.** People fly Dhaka to Dubai and on to Benghazi, are driven across Libya to Zuwara or Zawiya, and cross to the Italian island of Lampedusa in 24 to 36 hours <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>. The standard price is USD 10,000 (about Tk 12.4 lakh), USD 3,000 before leaving and USD 7,000 in Libya, and extra demands in Libya often raise it to USD 15,000 to 17,000 (about Tk 18.7 to 21.2 lakh) <sup>[N27](#n27), [1](#o1)</sup>. An "all-inclusive" package costs about USD 14,000 (about Tk 17.4 lakh), and in 2026 the Italian Embassy's migration expert said young Bangladeshis pay traffickers Tk 20 to 30 lakh for illegal routes to Italy <sup>[N27](#n27), [N42](#n42), [1](#o1)</sup>. Families have paid a broker Tk 18 to 20 lakh and then been asked Tk 25 lakh each in ransom <sup>[N43](#n43)</sup>. For many, the boat is the last stage of an attempt at legal migration <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>. On landing, people are taken to a hotspot (a first-reception centre) for fingerprints, and most Bangladeshi asylum claims are rejected (see [Irregular routes and undocumented workers](#irregular-routes-and-undocumented-workers); Asylum) <sup>[75](#o75), [N7](#n7)</sup>.

  > **Need to know**
  > - What does the Libya route to Italy cost in taka in late 2026, in Madaripur, Shariatpur, Sylhet or Cumilla, and what do brokers call the two instalments?

- **Fake Schengen visas: it ends at Rome airport.** In May 2026 two men who had agreed Tk 30 lakh each for fake Schengen visas, paying Tk 20 lakh up front, were caught at Rome airport and sent back, and the Biman ground officer who helped them board was arrested <sup>[N44](#n44)</sup>. Rings also paste a genuine Italian visa page into a look-alike's passport, or send another woman to Italy on the visa in a woman's passport <sup>[N45](#n45), [N46](#n46)</sup>.
- **Through another EU country:** one man paid an agency EUR 11,000 (about Tk 15.4 lakh) to go to Bucharest, in Romania, where he was offered undeclared building work, 12 hours a day, 7 days a week <sup>[N4](#n4), [1](#o1)</sup>. He refused, paid EUR 300 (about Tk 41,900) more for the Romanian nulla osta he needed to look for another job, then flew to Italy to join a cousin, since Romania is now in the Schengen area <sup>[N4](#n4), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Staying without a permit: it ends in a CPR, expulsion and a ban across Europe.** If police can't remove someone at once, the Questore (head of the Questura) can hold them in a CPR (Centro di Permanenza per i Rimpatri, a detention centre before removal) for three months, which a judge can extend <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>. An expulsion brings a 3 to 5-year ban recorded in the Schengen Information System, which bars every EU and Schengen country <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>. Italy returned over 120 Bangladeshis in 2025, and the Embassy warns that entering through dalals leads to immediate repatriation <sup>[77](#o77)</sup>. Workers who entered legally have ended there too: a Bangladeshi who came in 2023 was refused a permit by the Latina Questura, ordered expelled and held at Rome's Ponte Galeria CPR <sup>[N6](#n6)</sup>.

<a id="visas-not-open-to-bangladeshis"></a>

## 2 Visas not open to Bangladeshis

- **Visa-free entry:** Bangladeshis need a visa to enter Italy, and an airport transit visa even to change planes in an Italian airport <sup>[5](#o5), [78](#o78)</sup>.
- **Airport transit without a visa:** closed unless you already hold a valid Schengen or national visa or a residence permit, or fit another listed exemption <sup>[78](#o78)</sup>.
- **Working holiday visa (vacanze-lavoro):** only for citizens of New Zealand, Canada, Australia, Hong Kong, South Korea and Japan, the countries with an agreement with Italy <sup>[79](#o79)</sup>.
- **Turning a tourist visa into a stay or a job:** a tourist visa does not allow work, study or applying to stay in Italy (see [Changing visa type](#changing-visa-type)) <sup>[31](#o31)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has Italy suspended or limited any visa category for Bangladeshis in 2026, beyond the October 2024 suspension of old nulla osta?

<a id="work-visa-decreto-flussi-worker-non-seasonal"></a>

## 3 Work visa: Decreto Flussi worker (non-seasonal)

The Decreto Flussi ("flows decree") is Italy's yearly quota of foreign workers: an employer in Italy applies online for one named worker on a fixed "click day", the SUI (Sportello Unico per l'Immigrazione, the immigration desk at the Prefettura, the central government's office in each province) issues a nulla osta (work authorisation), and only then does the worker apply for a visa in Dhaka <sup>[8](#o8), [21](#o21)</sup>. Bangladesh is one of the countries with a cooperation agreement that share 25,000 reserved non-seasonal places a year in 2026, 2027 and 2028, inside a national non-seasonal quota of 76,200 <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>. About 10,000 Bangladeshis got Italian work visas in 2025, and the Embassy of Italy in Dhaka expects a similar number in 2026 <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-find-a-real-job"></a>

### 3.1 Step 1: Find a real job

A worker in Bangladesh can't apply for a nulla osta; only an Italian employer can, for a worker it names at its own choice, so every legal job starts with an employer who knows your name <sup>[N3](#n3), [N2](#n2)</sup>. There is no public list of workers and no list of certified agencies an employer must use <sup>[N2](#n2), [N4](#n4)</sup>. How names reach employers, and who sells them, is in Background (see [How a job reaches you](#how-a-job-reaches-you-and-who-takes-a-cut), and who takes a cut).

1. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-1"></a>**Register on the Overseas Employment Platform (OEP),** the website of BMET (the Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) for job-seekers <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>. BMET clears no one for work abroad without it, and the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare warns never to go without BMET clearance ([Step 6, item 2: Get BMET clearance (the smart card) yourself](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-2)) <sup>[73](#o73), [4](#o4)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[80](#o80), [3](#o3)</sup>:

   - your NID (national ID card);
   - a phone that can receive codes;
   - Tk 200 for the government fee.

   1. Open [employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/registration](https://employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/registration) and type your name in English exactly as on your NID, your mobile and NID numbers, and your date of birth, division and district as on the NID; type the captcha, accept the terms and press "যাচাই করুন" (Verify) <sup>[80](#o80)</sup>.
   2. Finish the form and pay the Tk 200 fee <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>.
   3. Take 2 printed copies of the registration slip to your DEMO (District Employment and Manpower Office, BMET's district office) to give your fingerprints <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>. DEMO addresses and phones are on BMET's list at [bmet.gov.bd](https://bmet.gov.bd/pages/static-pages/6922e139933eb65569e2af6c) <sup>[81](#o81)</sup>.

   **If it fails:** call the government's migrant call centre, 16135 (+880 9610102030 from abroad) <sup>[82](#o82)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-2"></a>**Know there is no government recruiter for Italy.** Bangladesh and Italy signed a Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) on Migration and Mobility in Dhaka in May 2025; it covers legal migration and the return of people with no right to stay <sup>[48](#o48)</sup>. Bangladesh asked Italy for larger quotas for skilled and semi-skilled workers in May 2025, and for Italian language and skills training in August 2026 <sup>[48](#o48), [N36](#n36)</sup>. A job in Italy still comes through an Italian employer's nulla osta ([Step 3: The employer gets your nulla osta](#step-3-the-employer-gets-your-nulla-osta)) <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Has BOESL (Bangladesh Overseas Employment and Services Limited, the state recruiter) ever posted an Italy job circular?
   > - Has the 2025 MoU's joint committee met, and has it given Bangladesh a share of the 25,000 reserved places?

3. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-3"></a>**Or train for a place outside the quota.** Under Article 23 of Italy's immigration law, a worker who completes a pre-departure course approved by Italy's Ministry of Labour (basic Italian, civic education and job skills) can enter outside the quota; the course can be done in Bangladesh, and applications run all year with no click day <sup>[N1](#n1), [83](#o83)</sup>. Several hundred Bangladeshis a year now go this way <sup>[12](#o12)</sup>. The visa steps for this route are in Work visa: highly skilled and out-of-quota (EU Blue Card, Article 27) <sup>[50](#o50)</sup>.

   - **The first projects in Bangladesh** were set up by Italian private firms: Frontiere Skills and Format, with about 300 students enrolled in November 2026 <sup>[28](#o28)</sup>. Frontiere Skills' TASTE project trains restaurant and hotel workers at Hotel Regency, Dhaka; Format trains textile workers and says its training is free <sup>[N1](#n1)</sup>.
   - **The EU Talent Partnership,** an EU-funded project (EUR 3 million, about Tk 41.9 crore, 2024 to 2027) run by BMET with the ILO (International Labour Organization), trains workers for EU jobs <sup>[N12](#n12), [1](#o1)</sup>. By July 2026, 698 trainees held NSDA (National Skills Development Authority) certificates, mainly for Italy, and many were waiting for employer interviews <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>. Its next phase adds Italian language training and a 10-day pre-departure orientation <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>.
   - **The Italian Embassy** said in October 2026 that it would hold a special event in Dhaka in November 2026 to recruit workers through language and skills training <sup>[N47](#n47)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How does a worker get a place on a Frontiere Skills or Format course: where to apply, what selection, and what is paid at each stage?
   > - How does a worker apply for a Talent Partnership course, at which TTCs (Technical Training Centres), and is it free?
   > - How many Talent Partnership graduates have received an Italian nulla osta?
   > - Where and when is the Embassy's November 2026 recruitment event, and who can attend?

4. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-4"></a>**If a relative, broker or agency brings you an employer, check the employer can legally hire you.** The nulla osta is refused or revoked if:

   - it got a nulla osta in the last three years and then did not sign the contratto di soggiorno (contract of stay), unless that was not its fault <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>;
   - it was convicted in the last five years of people smuggling, caporalato (illegal gangmaster labour) or employing undocumented workers <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>;
   - the documents were forged or obtained by fraud <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

   A private employer may file no more than three nulla osta requests a year; larger numbers must go through an employer organisation, a labour consultant or similar professional, or a temporary-work agency, who must keep them in line with the firm's turnover and staff <sup>[8](#o8), [49](#o49)</sup>. Networks have filed applications in the names of employers who did not know their names were used, and through a CAF (tax assistance centre) in Taranto with compliant employers <sup>[N22](#n22), [N23](#n23)</sup>. One worker who arrived for a company that did not exist fell into irregular status <sup>[N4](#n4)</sup>.

   Ask whoever brings the job for:

   - the employer's full name and address, and the job on offer, which go into its application ([Step 2, item 1: Get a named offer from the employer, not a "visa"](#step-2-the-job-offer-and-contract-item-1)) <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>;
   - the name of the labour consultant or employer organisation filing for it, if any <sup>[8](#o8), [84](#o84)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How can a worker in Bangladesh check that an Italian employer exists and is active (for example a visura camerale, the Chamber of Commerce registration extract, from registroimprese.it), and what does it cost?
   > - Can a worker ask a labour consultant or employer organisation in Italy to confirm it filed an application in the worker's name?

5. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-5"></a>**Know what you should pay.** Bangladesh's migration cost caps cover 14 countries, and Italy is not one of them; Italy sets no migration cost for the worker beyond the fees <sup>[13 (2019)](#o13), [N15 (2023)](#n15)</sup>. Pay any Bangladeshi agency only into its own bank account, against a receipt <sup>[54](#o54)</sup>. The Embassy tells anyone who uses an agency to check its reputation, take a receipt for every payment and know every document presented in their name <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. The official costs, each in the step that owns it:

   - OEP registration: Tk 200 ([Step 1, item 1: Register on the Overseas Employment Platform (OEP)](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-1)) <sup>[3](#o3)</sup>;
   - passport: Tk 4,025 to Tk 10,350 ([Step 4, item 1: Get an e-passport](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-1)) <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>;
   - apostille of certificates: Tk 200 a page ([Step 4, item 3: Apostille your certificates in Bangladesh, not at the Italian Embassy](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-3)) <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>;
   - police clearance, if asked: Tk 1,500 ([Step 4, item 2: Police clearance, if the Embassy or employer asks for it](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-2)) <sup>[87](#o87)</sup>;
   - the visa at VFS Global, the Embassy's visa application centre: Tk 21,830 ([Step 5, item 2: Submit the application at VFS](#step-5-the-visa-at-the-italian-embassy-in-dhaka-and-checking-it-is-real-item-2)) <sup>[2](#o2), [10](#o10)</sup>;
   - BMET clearance: Tk 5,500 welfare fee, Tk 1,000 insurance, Tk 250 clearance fee and Tk 250 or Tk 400 source tax ([Step 6, item 2: Get BMET clearance (the smart card) yourself](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight-item-2)) <sup>[53](#o53), [54](#o54), [4](#o4)</sup>;
   - in Italy, the residence permit application: EUR 116.46 or EUR 126.46 (about Tk 16,260 or Tk 17,650) ([Step 7, item 3: Apply for the permesso di soggiorno through the SUI and the post office](#step-7-after-arrival-item-3)) <sup>[88](#o88), [89](#o89), [1](#o1)</sup>.

   What people really pay, and to whom, is in Background (see What you pay, and what you earn and keep).

   > **Need to know**
   > - Who pays the flight to Italy under a Decreto Flussi contract in 2026, and what does a one-way Dhaka-Rome or Dhaka-Milan ticket cost?

6. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-6"></a>**If you need to borrow,** Probashi Kallyan Bank (PKB), the government bank for migrant workers, lends up to Tk 3 lakh to people aged 18 to 60 <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>. You first open a PKB savings account with at least Tk 500, and one of your guarantors must be a family member or close relative <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>. PKB helpline: 16238, or +880 9677-787878 from abroad <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does PKB lend against an Italian nulla osta, or only once the visa is stamped?

7. <a id="step-1-find-a-real-job-item-7"></a>**If a broker or agency takes your money,** complain in writing to BMET's Director General with your evidence; it is free and meant to be settled within 30 working days <sup>[4](#o4)</sup>. Hand it in at BMET, 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka-1000 <sup>[90 (2021)](#o90)</sup>. Then, in this order:

   1. BMET's Additional Director General (Employment): 02-48321949, 01720105492, adgemp@bmet.gov.bd <sup>[4](#o4)</sup>.
   2. The Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare's grievance officer: 02-41030224, 01712992086, adsadmin@probashi.gov.bd (30 working days) <sup>[91](#o91)</sup>.
   3. Call 16135, the Welfare Board's toll-free Probashi call centre <sup>[82](#o82)</sup>.

   Neither the Embassy nor VFS asks for money beyond the published fees; if either does, report it to the authorities <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does BMET accept a complaint against a broker who is not a licensed agency, for an Italy job with no BMET job order?
   > - Can a worker in Bangladesh report an Italian employer or consultant who sold a fake nulla osta to the Italian police (Guardia di Finanza or Carabinieri), and how?

<a id="step-2-the-job-offer-and-contract"></a>

### 3.2 Step 2: The job offer and contract

1. <a id="step-2-the-job-offer-and-contract-item-1"></a>**Get a named offer from the employer, not a "visa".** The employer's application to the SUI names you and must include <sup>[8](#o8), [55](#o55)</sup>:

   - a proposed contratto di soggiorno (contract of stay): the job's conditions, the employer's guarantee of housing and its promise to pay your journey home <sup>[8](#o8), [55](#o55)</sup>;
   - documents on where you will live, signed digitally; a hotel, or a fixed dormitory on a building site with the employer's own declaration, is accepted <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>;
   - an asseverazione: a certificate from a labour consultant or employer organisation that the employer follows the CCNL (contratto collettivo nazionale di lavoro, the national collective contract for its sector, which sets pay) and can afford the number of workers it asks for, judged on its assets, turnover and staff <sup>[84](#o84), [92 (2016)](#o92)</sup>.

   Minimum pay and hours under each CCNL are in Your rights at work <sup>[84](#o84)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Do Italian employers send the worker the proposed contratto di soggiorno before click day, or does the worker first see it after arrival?
   > - What job titles and monthly pay appear on the offers Bangladeshis actually receive in 2026 (construction, food industry, logistics, hotels)?

2. <a id="step-2-the-job-offer-and-contract-item-2"></a>**Give the employer exactly what is in your passport.** The visa is delayed if the name, surname, sex or date of birth on the nulla osta do not match your passport, so send a clear scan of the passport data page and check the spelling the employer types <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>. Get the passport first ([Step 4, item 1: Get an e-passport](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-1)) <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-2-the-job-offer-and-contract-item-3"></a>**Ask the employer to forward everything.** The employer must promptly tell you of every communication it receives about your nulla osta <sup>[21](#o21), [6](#o6)</sup>. You can send this message <sup>[21](#o21), [93](#o93)</sup>:

   "Dear [employer name], please send me (1) a copy of the nulla osta with its number and the SUI (Prefettura) that issued it, and (2) every email you receive from the Sportello Unico about my application. My name must be exactly as in my passport: [SURNAME], [Given names], born [DD/MM/YYYY], passport [number]. Thank you, [your name], [WhatsApp number]."

   **If it fails:** checks on a nulla osta are made in Italy, so without the employer's copies you can check little yourself ([Step 5: The visa at the Italian Embassy in Dhaka, and checking it is real](#step-5-the-visa-at-the-italian-embassy-in-dhaka-and-checking-it-is-real), "Check it is real") <sup>[94 (2024)](#o94), [21](#o21)</sup>. The Embassy tells applicants to know every document presented in their name <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>.

<a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-nulla-osta"></a>

### 3.3 Step 3: The employer gets your nulla osta

The employer, or its labour consultant or employer organisation, files the application on the Interior Ministry's ALI portal with the SUI (Sportello Unico per l'Immigrazione) of its province <sup>[21](#o21), [8](#o8)</sup>. The dates are for the 2027 round, the next one <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-nulla-osta-item-1"></a>**The employer checks the Italian labour market first.** It asks the Centro per l'Impiego (public job centre) whether a worker already in Italy is available; if the centre does not answer within 8 days, the check counts as negative and the employer may hire from abroad <sup>[95](#o95), [8](#o8)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-nulla-osta-item-2"></a>**The employer pre-fills the application** on the Portale Servizi ALI ([portaleservizi.dlci.interno.it](https://portaleservizi.dlci.interno.it/AliSportello/ali/home.htm)) from 9:00 on 23 October 2026 to 20:00 on 7 December 2026, on form B2020 for non-seasonal work <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>. Pre-filling is compulsory, and the authorities check the truth of the employer's declarations when it logs in <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>. From 9 to 13 December 2026 the portal reopens only to complete and save forms already started; no new applications can be added <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>. The INL (Ispettorato Nazionale del Lavoro, the labour inspectorate) may check pre-filled applications in advance, and can run random checks on employers with the Agenzia delle Entrate (tax agency) <sup>[6](#o6), [84](#o84)</sup>.

3. <a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-nulla-osta-item-3"></a>**The employer clicks "send" on click day.** For 2027 <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>:

   - non-seasonal work (form B2020): 16 February 2027, from 9:00;
   - domestic and care work (form A-bis): 18 February 2027 (see [Work visa: domestic and care worker](#work-visa-domestic-and-care-worker));
   - seasonal agriculture: 12 January 2027; seasonal tourism: 9 February 2027 (see [Work visa: seasonal worker](#work-visa-seasonal-worker)).

   Applications are handled mostly in order of arrival, and the quota is usually gone within the first ten minutes <sup>[50](#o50), [N3](#n3)</sup>. They can be sent until 31 December 2027 while quota remains <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>. The 2027 national figures are 76,200 non-seasonal places (14,000 of them for domestic and care work), 89,000 seasonal and 650 self-employed, 165,850 in all <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>.

4. <a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-nulla-osta-item-4"></a>**The quota is shared out, then the SUI decides.** The Ministry of Labour splits the quota by province within 10 days of click day <sup>[95](#o95), [50](#o50)</sup>. From that allocation the SUI has at most 60 days to issue the nulla osta <sup>[8](#o8), [50](#o50)</sup>. Applications that have not entered the quota six months after 31 December of the year lapse and are archived <sup>[96](#o96)</sup>. A nulla osta is valid for up to six months from issue <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.

5. <a id="step-3-the-employer-gets-your-nulla-osta-item-5"></a>**Know the extra checks on Bangladeshis.** For the 2026-2028 rounds, a nulla osta for a worker from Bangladesh, Pakistan, Sri Lanka or Morocco is never issued automatically when the time runs out: it always needs a favourable opinion from the Questura (the provincial police immigration office), and the INL first checks the employer <sup>[16](#o16)</sup>. The earlier rule in Decree-Law 145/2024 that singled out Bangladesh, Pakistan and Sri Lanka ran until 31 December 2025, pending a Foreign Ministry decree naming high-risk countries, and the law's text as updated to February 2026 does not extend it <sup>[15](#o15), [97](#o97)</sup>. Why, and what happened to the nulla osta frozen in October 2024, is in Background (see [Status](#status)) <sup>[14 (2024)](#o14)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Has the Foreign Ministry named high-risk countries, and is Bangladesh on the list in 2026?
   > - Do all Prefetture apply the Questura opinion and INL check to Bangladeshi non-seasonal nulla osta in 2026, as the Cosenza Prefettura's circular does?
   > - How does the employer see on ALI that its application "entered the quota", and what does the screen say?
   > - In 2026, how many B2020 applications for Bangladeshis were sent on click day, and how many got a nulla osta?
   > - How long after click day did Bangladeshis' 2026 nulla osta actually arrive?

**What you get back:** a copy of the nulla osta, which the employer gives you; you upload it when you register for the visa ([Step 5, item 1: Register on VFS's work-visa webform](#step-5-the-visa-at-the-italian-embassy-in-dhaka-and-checking-it-is-real-item-1)) <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.

<a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates"></a>

### 3.4 Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates

1. <a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-1"></a>**Get an e-passport** before the employer files, so the nulla osta carries your exact details ([Step 2, item 2: Give the employer exactly what is in your passport](#step-2-the-job-offer-and-contract-item-2)) <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>. Over age 20 you need your NID, and you fill in the form exactly as on it <sup>[98](#o98)</sup>. A 48-page passport costs <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>:

   - 5 years: Tk 4,025 (regular), Tk 6,325 (express), Tk 8,625 (super express);
   - 10 years: Tk 5,750, Tk 8,050 or Tk 10,350.

   1. Pay online through A-Challan, DGePay or ShurjoPay, or at any bank through A-Challan <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>.
   2. Enrol at the passport office; with an online-verified NID there is no police verification <sup>[99](#o99)</sup>.
   3. Delivery takes up to 15, 7 or 2 working days from enrolment (regular, express, super express) <sup>[85](#o85)</sup>.

   The visa needs a passport valid at least 3 months beyond the visa's end, with two blank visa pages <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>.

   **If it is lost or stolen:** file a GD (general diary, a police report) at the nearest police station quickly, then re-apply with a copy of the old passport and the GD <sup>[98](#o98)</sup>. Passport helpline: 16445 (09666716445 from abroad), 24 hours <sup>[100 (2023)](#o100)</sup>.

2. <a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-2"></a>**Police clearance, if the Embassy or employer asks for it.** You'll need a passport valid at least 3 months, and your present address must be one of your passport's addresses <sup>[87](#o87)</sup>.

   1. Apply at [pcc.police.gov.bd](https://pcc.police.gov.bd/) <sup>[87](#o87)</sup>.
   2. Pay Tk 1,500 by treasury challan, code 1-7301-0001-2681, at Bangladesh Bank or Sonali Bank, or online by card with a service charge <sup>[87](#o87)</sup>.
   3. Track it by texting PCC S and your reference number to 26969 <sup>[87](#o87)</sup>.

   Helpdesk: Sunday to Thursday, 9 am to 5 pm, 01320001824, 01320001825, help.pcc@police.gov.bd <sup>[87](#o87)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does the Italian Embassy in Dhaka require a police clearance for a Decreto Flussi work visa?

3. <a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-3"></a>**Apostille your certificates in Bangladesh, not at the Italian Embassy.** The visa needs documents or certificates for the job sector you will work in ([Step 5, item 2: Submit the application at VFS](#step-5-the-visa-at-the-italian-embassy-in-dhaka-and-checking-it-is-real-item-2)) <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>. Since June 2025 the Italian Embassy in Dhaka no longer legalises Bangladeshi public documents; they need an apostille (the international certificate under the Hague Apostille Convention) from Bangladesh's Ministry of Foreign Affairs (MoFA) through MyGov <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>. Italy accepts Bangladesh's apostille <sup>[102](#o102), [12](#o12)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[86](#o86), [103](#o103)</sup>:

   - a scan of each certificate (JPEG, JPG or PNG);
   - Tk 200 per page;
   - for SSC (Secondary School Certificate) and HSC (Higher Secondary Certificate): verification by your Education Board and the Ministry of Education first.

   1. Register on [mygov.bd](https://www.mygov.bd/), choose the attestation service for your document type, fill in the form and upload the document <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>.
   2. Pay Tk 200 per page online; it takes 5 working days <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>.
   3. An SMS comes when the apostille is issued; download it from your MyGov account <sup>[86](#o86)</sup>.

   A birth certificate is first notarised, then attested by the Law Ministry, then by MoFA <sup>[104](#o104)</sup>. The Embassy accepts only one apostille per document <sup>[105](#o105), [106](#o106)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Must experience letters be apostilled for a Decreto Flussi visa, or only for the Blue Card?

4. <a id="step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-4"></a>**Translate into Italian with an Embassy-authorised translator, if asked.** VFS Global takes all translation and legalisation requests, and legalising an e-apostille is free <sup>[101](#o101), [107](#o107), [108](#o108)</sup>. The Embassy's authorised translators are <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>:

   - Tradusoft.com, 136 Link Road, Middle Badda, Dhaka; +8801776015230;
   - EXINSO Ltd., House 63/4 (1st floor), Progoti Sharani, North Badda, Dhaka 1212; +8801797-412712;
   - Prof. Stefania Chiapello; +8801755596360.

   The Embassy's 2026 fee list, at EUR 1 = Tk 140, charges EUR 37 (Tk 5,180) each for a police clearance or a study certificate <sup>[108](#o108)</sup>. A dichiarazione di valore (the Embassy's statement of what a foreign qualification is worth in Italy) is EUR 50 (Tk 7,000), free for study in Italy <sup>[108](#o108)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What does the Embassy's EUR 37 fee for a police clearance or study certificate pay for: translation, legalisation or a certificate?
   > - Does a Decreto Flussi worker ever need a dichiarazione di valore, or only for the Blue Card and study?

   **Scams:** Embassy appointments are always free, including through VFS; anyone selling a slot is not the Embassy <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>. Forging documents, signatures or seals is prosecuted <sup>[101](#o101)</sup>.

<a id="step-5-the-visa-at-the-italian-embassy-in-dhaka-and-checking-it-is-real"></a>

### 3.5 Step 5: The visa at the Italian Embassy in Dhaka, and checking it is real

All Italian visa applications in Bangladesh go through VFS Global, never to the Embassy directly; VFS handles only the logistics and has no say in decisions or document checks <sup>[10](#o10), [11](#o11)</sup>. VFS centres take applications Sunday to Thursday, 9:00 to 12:00 <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>:

- Dhaka: Nafi Tower (4th and 5th floor), 53 South Gulshan Avenue, Gulshan 1 <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>;
- Chattogram: World Trade Center, 102/103 Agrabad CA, Commerce College Road <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>;
- Sylhet: Nirvana Inn Complex (4th floor), Ramer Dighir Par, Mirzajungle Road <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>;
- help: info.itbd@vfshelpline.com, +880 9606777666 <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

1. <a id="step-5-the-visa-at-the-italian-embassy-in-dhaka-and-checking-it-is-real-item-1"></a>**Register on VFS's work-visa webform** as soon as you have the nulla osta <sup>[109](#o109), [24](#o24)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[24](#o24), [110](#o110)</sup>:

   - your own email address;
   - a copy of the nulla osta, which the employer in Italy can provide.

   1. Open [vfseu.mioot.com/forms/ITABDE](https://vfseu.mioot.com/forms/ITABDE/), enter your email and the captcha, and press "Get One Time Password" <sup>[110](#o110), [23](#o23)</sup>.
   2. Enter the code from your email, fill in the form and upload the nulla osta; the same login later shows the status of your application <sup>[110](#o110), [24](#o24)</sup>.
   3. Registering stops the nulla osta from expiring while you wait for an appointment <sup>[109](#o109)</sup>.
   4. The Embassy checks the nulla osta and then gives you an appointment, within a few days for a 2025 nulla osta <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>. Holders of a 2023 or 2024 nulla osta who already applied wait until the immigration office in Italy finishes its review, after which VFS contacts them <sup>[24](#o24)</sup>.

   Give your own phone and email, so VFS reaches you directly and not through an intermediary <sup>[11](#o11)</sup>. Before the webform, in 2024, workers waited six to seven months after the nulla osta for an appointment, and local brokers took money promising to guarantee one <sup>[N20 (2024)](#n20)</sup>. An L/N nulla osta (Article 27, outside the quota) follows a walk-in route instead (see Work visa: highly skilled and out-of-quota (EU Blue Card, Article 27)) <sup>[111](#o111), [29](#o29)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - In 2026, how many days pass between registering a nulla osta on the webform and getting the appointment email?
   > - Do brokers still sell "guaranteed" VFS work-visa appointments in Dhaka in 2026, now that the Embassy gives them from the webform, and for how much?

2. <a id="step-5-the-visa-at-the-italian-embassy-in-dhaka-and-checking-it-is-real-item-2"></a>**Submit the application at VFS.** You'll need <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>:

   - the original national ("D") visa form, filled in in English or Italian and signed;
   - one photo, 3.5 x 4.0 cm, colour, white background, taken in the last 6 months;
   - your passport, valid at least 3 months beyond the visa's end, with two blank visa pages, and a copy of the data page;
   - a copy of the nulla osta (check name, surname, sex and date of birth);
   - documents or certificates for the job sector you will work in, such as experience letters or training certificates ([Step 4, item 3: Apostille your certificates in Bangladesh, not at the Italian Embassy](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates-item-3));
   - anything else the Embassy asks for.

   You give fingerprints at VFS; they are compulsory for every work visa applicant <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>. You may file only one visa application at a time, even with two passports <sup>[93](#o93)</sup>. Pay in cash, in taka, at the Embassy's rate of EUR 1 = Tk 140 <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>:

   - the visa fee: Tk 16,240 (EUR 116) <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>;
   - the VFS service charge: Tk 5,320 (EUR 38), VAT included <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>;
   - a bank draft charge: Tk 270 <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>.

   That is Tk 21,830 in all <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>. The VFS charge already covers SMS and email notices, photocopies, the photo booth and help filling in the form <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which work-visa applicants pay the lower fee of Tk 12,600 (EUR 90) that VFS also lists <sup>[2](#o2)</sup>?
   > - What do Dhaka agents charge in 2026 to fill in the form or "manage" the file, on top of the Tk 21,830?

3. <a id="step-5-the-visa-at-the-italian-embassy-in-dhaka-and-checking-it-is-real-item-3"></a>**The employer must confirm within 15 days.** When the Embassy finishes its checks, the SUI emails the employer by PEC (posta elettronica certificata, certified email) asking it to confirm it still wants to hire you; if it does not confirm within 15 days the request counts as refused and the nulla osta is revoked <sup>[8](#o8), [93](#o93), [50](#o50), [21](#o21)</sup>. Only after this confirmation does the Embassy issue the visa <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>. Tell the employer to watch its PEC inbox once you have applied <sup>[21](#o21)</sup>.

4. <a id="step-5-the-visa-at-the-italian-embassy-in-dhaka-and-checking-it-is-real-item-4"></a>**Follow the application and collect the passport.** If the Embassy wants more documents, VFS's passport tracker shows it with a deadline, and a wrong phone number or email is your loss <sup>[112](#o112)</sup>. Collect the passport within 90 days, or it goes back to the Embassy and on to Bangladesh's Ministry of Home Affairs <sup>[112](#o112)</sup>. To take your passport back before a decision, ask VFS in person with the signed withdrawal form, a copy of a valid ID and the VFS receipt <sup>[107](#o107)</sup>. By law the Embassy issues a Decreto Flussi work visa within 30 days of the application, but the deadline does not hold when checks with foreign authorities are needed <sup>[113](#o113)</sup>. Consulates also run their own checks and may ask for more documents or an interview <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>. Across all consulates the wait for issue averaged 18 days in the 2025 round, but Dhaka took 290 days on average for the files suspended in 2024, and applicants from Bangladesh and the other countries under extra checks have waited up to almost ten months <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>. A refusal comes without advance warning, so you cannot add missing papers first; it must give reasons, in a language you understand or else in English, French, Spanish or Arabic <sup>[N11](#n11), [114](#o114), [115](#o115)</sup>. You can appeal to the TAR (Tribunale Amministrativo Regionale, the regional administrative court) of Lazio within 60 days of notification, serving the appeal also on the Avvocatura dello Stato (the State Legal Service) <sup>[114](#o114), [115](#o115)</sup>. TAR appeals against a revoked nulla osta or visa can cost thousands of euros <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>. A visa already issued is revoked if facts come to light that would have barred it <sup>[113](#o113)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How many working days from VFS submission to visa does the Dhaka Embassy take in 2026 for a new (2025 or later) Decreto Flussi nulla osta?


**Check it is real:**

1. <a id="work-visa-decreto-flussi-worker-non-seasonal-item-1"></a>**Your details.** The nulla osta, issued by the SUI, must carry your exact name, surname, sex and date of birth <sup>[93](#o93), [8](#o8)</sup>.
2. <a id="work-visa-decreto-flussi-worker-non-seasonal-item-2"></a>**The Embassy's check.** The Embassy verifies every nulla osta registered on the webform before it gives an appointment; in May 2025 it said over 70% of webform entries carried fake or invalid nulla osta <sup>[24](#o24), [11](#o11)</sup>.
3. <a id="work-visa-decreto-flussi-worker-non-seasonal-item-3"></a>**Ask the employer for the SUI's emails.** Verification of a nulla osta is done in Italy, and the Embassy tells holders to ask their employer for updates <sup>[94 (2024)](#o94), [21](#o21)</sup>. Official messages about the file usually go only to the employer, and there is no public portal to check a nulla osta by its number <sup>[N11](#n11)</sup>.
4. <a id="work-visa-decreto-flussi-worker-non-seasonal-item-4"></a>**Ask the Embassy in writing** at visti.dhaka@esteri.it, attaching the nulla osta <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.
5. <a id="work-visa-decreto-flussi-worker-non-seasonal-item-5"></a>**The webform status** on vfseu.mioot.com shows your application's progress, and VFS notices come by SMS and email <sup>[110](#o110), [2](#o2)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a worker check an Italian visa sticker's number online, or only by writing to visti.dhaka@esteri.it?
> - What does a forged nulla osta sold in Dhaka typically get wrong (protocol number, SUI name, QR code)?

**If any check fails:** complain as in [Step 1, item 7: If a broker or agency takes your money](#step-1-find-a-real-job-item-7) <sup>[4](#o4)</sup>.
