   **Apply early:** a claim made without good reason more than 90 days after entering irregularly, or after your permit expired, goes into the accelerated procedure and gives no right to a reception place <sup>[N66](#n66), [246](#o246)</sup>. **Only one EU country examines your claim,** generally the first one you entered, and you cannot choose <sup>[N61](#n61)</sup>.

   **If it fails:** in big cities registration can take weeks or months after you ask, and a Questura that turns people away gives them no paper, which leaves them open to arrest and deportation <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>. In 2024, waits of 90 to 100 days were recorded at the Questure of Vicenza and Venice <sup>[N67](#n67)</sup>. For help, in this order:
   1. The free legal service at your reception centre, or a free NGO legal desk <sup>[N61](#n61)</sup>.
   2. The ARCI-UNHCR helpline for refugees and asylum seekers: 800 905 570, or +39 351 137 6335 on WhatsApp or Lycamobile, numeroverderifugiati@arci.it <sup>[N68](#n68)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - Who registers asylum claims from 1 November 2026, now that the Questure's transitional role has ended?
   > - Where is the Bengali "Practical guide" published online?
   > - Which Questure in Rome, Milan, Naples and Venice take asylum claims by online booking, and how long is the wait in late 2026?
   > - Does the 90-day late-claim rule still apply now that Decree-Law 100/2026 repealed most of Article 28-bis of the Procedure Decree?

2. <a id="how-to-apply-item-2"></a>**Go to the interview at the Territorial Commission.** Only the Commission judges your claim; the police cannot <sup>[N66](#n66)</sup>. A Bangla interpreter must be present <sup>[244](#o244)</sup>. You must attend, state every relevant fact and hand over all your documents, including your passport <sup>[244](#o244)</sup>. Missing the interview can count as withdrawing your claim <sup>[244](#o244)</sup>.
   - Tell the Questura and the Commission at once of any change of address; otherwise letters, including the decision, count as delivered to your last declared address <sup>[244](#o244)</sup>.
   - You may bring a lawyer at your own cost; none is needed at this stage <sup>[244](#o244), [N61](#n61)</sup>.
   - What you say is confidential and is not shared with Bangladeshi authorities without your consent <sup>[N61](#n61)</sup>.
   - For people rescued at sea, caught crossing irregularly or held in a CPR, the Questore (head of the Questura) can order a check of their phone and SIM to establish identity, nationality and route; messages are excluded <sup>[244](#o244)</sup>.

   **Time limits:** a decision is due within 8 months in the ordinary procedure, 4 months in the accelerated procedure and four weeks in the border procedure <sup>[N69](#n69), [241](#o241)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - How long are Bangladeshis waiting in late 2026 between registration and the Commission interview in Rome, Milan and Naples?

<a id="while-you-wait-reception-work-and-papers"></a>

### 13.3 While you wait: reception, work and papers

1. <a id="while-you-wait-reception-work-and-papers-item-1"></a>**Reception.** You get a reception place only if you ask for it when you apply, and you cannot choose the town <sup>[N68](#n68)</sup>. Most people are placed in CAS (Centri di Accoglienza Straordinaria, emergency reception centres), which held 101,106 people on 31 December 2025 <sup>[N65](#n65)</sup>. Pocket money is EUR 2.50 (about Tk 350) a day, and there is no allowance outside the system <sup>[N70](#n70), [1](#o1)</sup>. Italian lessons and job orientation are optional services <sup>[N70](#n70)</sup>. You lose the place if you leave without permission, are violent or have enough money of your own <sup>[N68](#n68)</sup>. If the centre's manager does not fix a problem, complain to the local Prefettura (the central government's office in each province) <sup>[N68](#n68)</sup>.
2. <a id="while-you-wait-reception-work-and-papers-item-2"></a>**Work.** You may work once 90 days have passed since your claim was formalised, if no decision has been made and the delay is not your fault <sup>[247](#o247)</sup>. The asylum permit cannot be turned into a work permit, even if you have a job <sup>[N60](#n60)</sup>. Employers are often wary of hiring someone who holds only an asylum receipt <sup>[N60](#n60)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Under Decree-Law 100/2026, what permit does an asylum seeker hold after formalisation, for how long, and is it renewed at the Questura or the Prefettura?

<a id="the-decision"></a>

### 13.4 The decision

The Commission can grant refugee status or subsidiary protection (both with 5-year permits), find that special protection applies (a 2-year permit), or reject the claim <sup>[N65](#n65), [N39](#n39)</sup>. For Bangladeshis in January to September 2026, 375 got refugee status and 3,255 special protection, out of 27,790 decisions <sup>[236](#o236)</sup>.

**Special protection (protezione speciale):** a national permit for people who cannot be expelled because they risk persecution, torture or inhuman treatment, or because of Italy's constitutional and international obligations <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>. Many workers caught by fake job contracts try to regularise through it <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>. Since Decree-Law 20/2023, it can no longer be asked for directly at the Questura, and your private and family life in Italy no longer counts as a ground <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>. The permit lasts two years and is renewable, but can no longer be converted into a work permit <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>. Renewals can take 16 to 18 months <sup>[N65](#n65)</sup>.

Victims of labour exploitation can get a separate "special cases" permit (see [Caporalato: illegal gangmasters](#caporalato-illegal-gangmasters)) <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>.

<a id="if-it-is-refused"></a>

### 13.5 If it is refused

The rejection is also a return order and an entry ban, with the effect of an expulsion order, and is challenged in the same appeal <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>.

1. <a id="if-it-is-refused-item-1"></a>**Appeal within the deadline.** It runs from the day the decision is notified:
   - ordinary procedure: 30 days (60 if you are abroad) <sup>[N69](#n69)</sup>;
   - accelerated cases under Article 67(7)(a) of the EU Asylum Procedure Regulation: 7 days <sup>[241](#o241)</sup>;
   - border procedure: 5 days <sup>[241](#o241)</sup>.

   The appeal goes to the specialised immigration section of the civil court (tribunale) <sup>[N66](#n66)</sup>. A lawyer is required, and people without money have the right to a free, state-paid lawyer (patrocinio a spese dello Stato) <sup>[N61](#n61), [244](#o244)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-it-is-refused-item-2"></a>**Ask to stay while the court decides, where needed.** As a rule an appeal stops removal, except in the cases set by Article 68(3) of the Regulation <sup>[241](#o241)</sup>. In the border procedure it does not, except for unaccompanied children, so file a request to stay with the appeal; you cannot be removed while the judge decides it <sup>[241](#o241)</sup>. A second or later repeat claim gives no right to stay <sup>[N69](#n69)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-it-is-refused-item-3"></a>**Wait for the court.** Appeals often take far longer than the 4 months the law sets, in some cases ten times longer <sup>[N66](#n66)</sup>. After the civil court, only a challenge on points of law at the Court of Cassation remains, within 30 days <sup>[N66](#n66)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What share of Bangladeshi asylum appeals succeed at the civil courts in Rome, Milan, Bologna and Naples in 2025-2026?

<a id="after-a-final-refusal"></a>

### 13.6 After a final refusal

The return order and entry ban stand, with the effect of an expulsion order (see [What can get you jailed or deported](#what-can-get-you-jailed-or-deported)) <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>. On 25 September 2025 an EU-chartered flight returned 29 Bangladeshis, 16 of them from Italy <sup>[N71](#n71)</sup>. Moving on to another EU country does not help: only one country examines the claim, and the EU rules from June 2026 close that option <sup>[N61](#n61), [N71](#n71), [20](#o20)</sup>. For ways to leave, see [Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers).

<a id="scams-4"></a>

### 13.7 Scams

- **"Political asylum is easy in Italy."** In 2025, 26,240 of 29,595 Bangladeshi claims were rejected at first decision <sup>[N7](#n7)</sup>. The Italian Ambassador said in 2025 that people paid brokers the equivalent of about EUR 15,000 (about Tk 21 lakh) each to reach Italy <sup>[N63](#n63), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Fake papers and bought stories.** Rights groups warn that many Bangladeshi claims may now be dismissed as manifestly unfounded at the outset <sup>[N72](#n72)</sup>. Giving false information or documents puts you straight into the compulsory border procedure <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>.
- **Paying for what is free.** The interpreter, legal advice at reception centres and many NGOs, and a court lawyer for people without money are free <sup>[N61](#n61)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What do Bangladeshi "case-makers" in Rome (Torpignattara) or Milan charge in 2026 to write an asylum story or file an appeal, and are any under investigation?
> - Do intermediaries sell fake party membership cards, FIRs (First Information Reports, police case papers) or arrest warrants for Italian asylum claims, and at what price?

<a id="irregular-routes-and-undocumented-workers"></a>

## 14 Irregular routes and undocumented workers

The main irregular way into Italy for Bangladeshis is a legal flight to Benghazi in eastern Libya, then a boat to Italy <sup>[N27](#n27), [N73](#n73)</sup>. Bangladeshis are the largest nationality landing in Italy by sea: 6,160 of 27,242 people from 1 January to 6 November 2026, 20,164 of 66,296 in 2025, and 13,779 in 2024 <sup>[46](#o46), [18](#o18), [248 (2024)](#o248)</sup>. Italy's Ambassador in Dhaka warns that irregular entrants are very likely to be sent back, lose their money and may face future limits on travel to Europe <sup>[52](#o52)</sup>. What brokers charge is under Irregular routes, and where an irregular stay ends.

<a id="the-libya-sea-route"></a>

### 14.1 The Libya sea route

<a id="how-the-route-works"></a>

#### 14.1.1 How the route works

1. <a id="how-the-route-works-item-1"></a>**Recruitment in Bangladesh.** A village broker, or a relative abroad, promises Italy; the deal is often presented as legal migration, and irregular migration is often the last stage of a failed legal attempt <sup>[N27](#n27), [N74](#n74)</sup>. Loans are easier to get when the money is said to be for Italy <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>.
2. <a id="how-the-route-works-item-2"></a>**The flight.** Most fly Dhaka to Dubai, then to Benghazi directly or through Egypt; one man was routed through Chennai (India), Sri Lanka, Dubai and Alexandria (Egypt) to Benghazi <sup>[N27](#n27), [N74](#n74)</sup>. Some are given USD 500 (about Tk 62,000) in cash to hand to the facilitator in Dubai <sup>[N27](#n27), [1](#o1)</sup>.
3. <a id="how-the-route-works-item-3"></a>**Entry to Libya.** People enter Benghazi legally, on entry clearances from the Libyan National Army's Military Investment Authority (the armed force that controls eastern Libya) <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>. A local sponsor is needed at Benghazi airport, and migrants describe a special queue for Bangladeshis <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>.
4. <a id="how-the-route-works-item-4"></a>**Across Libya.** Drivers move them west through Ajdabiya, Sirte and Shwerayif to Zuwara and Zawiya, the main departure points since 2023 <sup>[N27](#n27), [N73](#n73)</sup>.
5. <a id="how-the-route-works-item-5"></a>**The boat.** The crossing to Lampedusa, the Italian island closest to Libya, takes 24 to 36 hours in fair weather <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>.

<a id="what-goes-wrong-in-libya"></a>

#### 14.1.2 What goes wrong in Libya

- **"Game houses"** (the last holding sites before the boat): smugglers demand more money even from people told their trip was fully paid <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>.
- **Ransom videos:** families receive calls, photos or videos of their relative being tortured, with instructions to send money through informal channels <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>. In July 2026 the families of six young men from Manoharganj, Cumilla, received such videos by imo and WhatsApp and were asked Tk 25 lakh each <sup>[N43](#n43)</sup>.
- **Staged kidnappings:** Bangladeshi and Libyan smugglers stage kidnappings together to force new payments; travellers on the cheaper "normal" deal are told they must pay their own release <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>.
- **Being sold:** people told they can pay after reaching Europe are made to work in Libya to repay the debt; in one case seven men were sold for USD 17,000 (about Tk 21 lakh) and the new handler asked USD 12,000 (about Tk 15 lakh) each <sup>[N27](#n27), [1](#o1)</sup>. Others are promised a "next visa" to Italy and temporary work in Libya, and have their passports taken on arrival <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>.
- **Caught at sea:** people intercepted by the Libyan Coast Guard report being handed to Bangladeshi networks who demand more money <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>.
- **Getting out:** the Bangladesh Embassy in Tripoli struggles to get Libyan exit permits; in 2023 it could not bring home about 2,000 people <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>. Returns do happen with IOM (International Organization for Migration, the UN migration agency): 175 people came home from Libya on 26 February 2026 <sup>[N75](#n75)</sup>.

<a id="deaths-at-sea"></a>

#### 14.1.3 Deaths at sea

In 2024, 1,718 people died or went missing crossing the central Mediterranean towards Italy, one for every 39 arrivals <sup>[N73](#n73)</sup>. IOM counted about 765 deaths there from 1 January to 7 April 2026, over 460 more than in the same period of 2025 <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>. In early April 2026 more than 80 people went missing when a boat from Tajoura, Libya, capsized <sup>[N76](#n76)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many Bangladeshis died or went missing on the central Mediterranean in 2024, 2025 and 2026?
> - What are the street names in Bangla for the Libya route and for the "game house"?

<a id="if-a-relative-is-held-for-ransom-in-libya"></a>

#### 14.1.4 If a relative is held for ransom in Libya

What families have done <sup>[N43](#n43), [N74](#n74)</sup>:

1. <a id="if-a-relative-is-held-for-ransom-in-libya-item-1"></a>**Applied in writing to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Dhaka,** asking for rescue, as the Cumilla families did in July 2026 <sup>[N43](#n43)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-a-relative-is-held-for-ransom-in-libya-item-2"></a>**Filed a case at the local police station.** In a Madaripur case, a case with Turag Police led the PBI (Police Bureau of Investigation) to arrest three suspects, and the man was freed <sup>[N74](#n74)</sup>. In the Cumilla case the local police said no written complaint had been filed <sup>[N43](#n43)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-a-relative-is-held-for-ransom-in-libya-item-3"></a>**Worked with the Bangladesh Embassy in Libya and IOM,** which organise return flights with the Foreign and Expatriates' Welfare ministries <sup>[N75](#n75)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What are the Bangladesh Embassy in Tripoli's emergency phone and WhatsApp numbers?
> - Does IOM Libya still take Bangladeshis for voluntary return in 2026, and how does a family register a relative?
> - Under which law (the Prevention and Suppression of Human Trafficking Act 2012 or the Overseas Employment and Migrants Act 2013) and at which court should a family file?

<a id="landing-lampedusa-and-the-hotspots"></a>

### 14.2 Landing: Lampedusa and the hotspots

1. <a id="landing-lampedusa-and-the-hotspots-item-1"></a>**Hotspot.** People rescued at sea or caught crossing are taken to a hotspot (punto di crisi) for first aid, health checks, photos and fingerprints <sup>[75](#o75)</sup>.
2. <a id="landing-lampedusa-and-the-hotspots-item-2"></a>**Screening.** Identity checks, fingerprints and transmission to Eurodac must end within 72 hours <sup>[75](#o75)</sup>. Refusing identification can mean being held up to 28 days at the hotspot or in a CPR <sup>[75](#o75)</sup>.
3. <a id="landing-lampedusa-and-the-hotspots-item-3"></a>**Information.** Staff must explain the border procedure, asylum, relocation and assisted voluntary return <sup>[75](#o75)</sup>. What follows an asylum claim is under Asylum.

> **Need to know**
> - How many days do Bangladeshis spend at the Lampedusa hotspot in 2026, and where are they sent next?

<a id="living-without-papers-in-italy"></a>

### 14.3 Living without papers in Italy

- **It is an offence:** entering or staying without permission is punished by a fine of EUR 5,000 to 10,000 (about Tk 7 to 14 lakh); expulsion is under What can get you jailed or deported <sup>[249 (2024)](#o249), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Health care:** people without papers get urgent or essential outpatient and hospital care, including continuing care, in public and accredited facilities <sup>[250 (2017)](#o250)</sup>. Going to a hospital cannot lead to a report to the police, except where an Italian would also be reported <sup>[250 (2017)](#o250)</sup>.
- **Work:** an employer who hires a worker without a valid permit faces 6 months to 3 years in prison and EUR 5,000 (about Tk 7 lakh) per worker <sup>[251](#o251), [1](#o1)</sup>.
- **Losing a permit:** people who lost a regular job, and with it their permit, have been detained and sent to the centre in Albania <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>.
- **Regularisation:** the 2020 emersione (regularisation) took 207,542 employer applications, for domestic and care work or farming and fishing only <sup>[74 (2020)](#o74)</sup>. Bangladeshis filed 16,102 domestic and care applications, the second most after Ukrainians, and 2,222 in farming <sup>[74 (2020)](#o74)</sup>; most applied as caregivers <sup>[N77 (2020)](#n77)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many of the 18,324 Bangladeshi 2020 emersione applications ended in a permit, and how many were still pending in 2025?
> - Can a Bangladeshi without a permit renew a passport at the Embassy in Rome or the Consulate General in Milan?
> - How do undocumented Bangladeshis get the STP card (Straniero Temporaneamente Presente, the health code for foreigners without papers), and at which counters in Rome and Milan?

<a id="detention-in-a-cpr"></a>

### 14.4 Detention in a CPR

Police hold people awaiting deportation in a CPR <sup>[245](#o245), [76](#o76)</sup>. Italy had 11 CPRs with 883 places and 6,835 entries in 2025, and plans six more <sup>[N78](#n78)</sup>. Bangladeshis are among those held <sup>[N79](#n79)</sup>.

- **How long:** a judge must validate detention, which then lasts 3 months; it can be extended by 3 months, then in 3-month steps up to 12 more months <sup>[245](#o245)</sup>.
- **Legal help:** at the hearing that validates an expulsion, you can get free legal aid and an interpreter <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>.
- **Who is detained:** having no valid passport counts as a "risk of flight", which allows removal by police escort <sup>[252](#o252)</sup>.
- **Voluntary return from inside:** a detainee admitted to assisted voluntary return stays in the CPR until departure ([Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers), [Step 2, item 1: Apply for assisted voluntary return (AVR)](#step-2-choose-how-to-leave-item-1)) <sup>[253](#o253)</sup>.
- **If not removed:** if you cannot be held or removed, the Questore orders you in writing to leave within 7 days <sup>[245](#o245)</sup>. Ignoring the order can bring a fine of EUR 10,000 to 20,000 (about Tk 14 to 28 lakh), or EUR 6,000 to 15,000 (about Tk 8.4 to 21 lakh) if the expulsion came with time to leave voluntarily <sup>[245](#o245), [1](#o1)</sup>.

<a id="the-albania-centre"></a>

#### 14.4.1 The Albania centre

Since a decree of 28 March 2025, people already in a CPR in Italy can be moved to the CPR at Gjader, Albania, run by Italy <sup>[N38](#n38)</sup>. About 90 were held there in late February 2026, and no more than 700 had been transferred by August 2026 <sup>[N38](#n38), [N80](#n80)</sup>. Detainees share a single phone, and anyone to be deported must first be flown back to Italy <sup>[N81](#n81), [N38](#n38)</sup>. In August 2025 a Rome court gave international protection to several Bangladeshis who had been held at Gjader, among them a man tortured for ransom in Libya <sup>[N82](#n82)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many Bangladeshis were held in Italian CPRs and at Gjader in 2025 and 2026?
> - How does a family in Bangladesh find out which CPR a relative is in?

<a id="deportation-to-bangladesh"></a>

### 14.5 Deportation to Bangladesh

Italy and Bangladesh signed an MoU (Memorandum of Understanding) on Migration and Mobility in Dhaka on 5-6 May 2025, covering returns under the 2017 Bangladesh-EU Standard Operating Procedures for returning people with no right to stay <sup>[254](#o254), [48](#o48)</sup>. About 5,000 Bangladeshis have been returned from Europe under those procedures, many from Italy <sup>[N83](#n83)</sup>.

- Over 120 Bangladeshis were returned from Italy in 2025, to early November <sup>[77](#o77)</sup>.
- On 31 October 2025 Italy returned 4 Bangladeshis, 2 of whom had arrived from Libya without a visa a month before; they were arrested around Rome and flown from Fiumicino <sup>[77](#o77), [N84](#n84)</sup>.
- The Italian Embassy in Dhaka warns that paying traffickers, brokers and dalals (middlemen) to enter without a proper visa, or with a fraudulent one, leads to immediate repatriation <sup>[77](#o77)</sup>.
- On 23 October 2026 a charter flight brought 19 Bangladeshis from Italy, with 10 from Greece and 6 from Cyprus <sup>[N83](#n83)</sup>.
- At Dhaka airport, BRAC's Migration Programme meets returned people with emergency help, counselling and money to get home, then training and financial help (see [Coming home](#coming-home)) <sup>[N83](#n83)</sup>.
- Italy carried out 6,443 forced returns and 1,063 assisted voluntary returns of all nationalities from 1 January to 4 November 2026 <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Are deportees from Italy questioned or charged by Bangladesh police or immigration at Dhaka airport?
> - Does the Bangladesh Embassy in Rome issue travel documents for deportation, and how long does identification take?

<a id="scams-5"></a>

### 14.6 Scams

- **"Pay later, in Italy."** Debts promised "after arrival" become forced work in Libya (see [What goes wrong in Libya](#what-goes-wrong-in-libya)) <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>.
- **"Premium" journeys.** The promise that the smuggler pays any ransom is used to sell a higher price; cheaper travellers face staged kidnappings <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>.
- **Italy-based contacts.** A man was introduced through a relative to an Italy-based expatriate and a local agent who promised legal migration on a Tk 24 lakh deal, and was later taken to Tripoli and held for ransom <sup>[N74](#n74)</sup>. In February 2025 Italian anti-mafia police arrested a man at Fiumicino airport after victims testified that his network held people hostage in Libya while his brother collected ransom in Bangladesh <sup>[N85](#n85)</sup>; that Madaripur-based network charged about EUR 11,000 (about Tk 15.4 lakh) a person <sup>[N85](#n85), [1](#o1)</sup>.

<a id="going-home-without-valid-papers"></a>

## 15 Going home without valid papers

Without a valid permit there are three ways out of Italy: assisted voluntary return (AVR), leaving on your own, or deportation <sup>[255](#o255), [252](#o252)</sup>. AVR is free: it pays for the travel document, the ticket and a cash grant, and admission suspends any expulsion order <sup>[255](#o255), [253](#o253)</sup>. Leaving on your own means no forced expulsion, but you can still be given a re-entry ban of 1 to 3 years <sup>[252](#o252)</sup>. Deportation brings a ban of 3 to 5 years <sup>[252](#o252)</sup>.

<a id="step-1-get-a-passport-or-travel-permit"></a>

### 15.1 Step 1: Get a passport or Travel Permit

With a valid Bangladeshi passport, go to [Step 2: Choose how to leave](#step-2-choose-how-to-leave). If it has expired or been lost, get a Travel Permit (a one-way document to return home) from the Embassy of Bangladesh in Rome (address under Help from the Bangladesh Embassy in Rome and Consulate in Milan), which charges EUR 28 (about Tk 3,900) <sup>[232](#o232), [1](#o1)</sup>. Under AVR the programme pays for it <sup>[255](#o255)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What documents does the Embassy in Rome ask for a Travel Permit (NID, old passport copy, photos, a police report for a lost passport)?
> - How many days does a Travel Permit take in Rome and in Milan, and does Milan charge the same fee?

<a id="step-2-choose-how-to-leave"></a>

### 15.2 Step 2: Choose how to leave

1. <a id="step-2-choose-how-to-leave-item-1"></a>**Apply for assisted voluntary return (AVR).** Italy's national AVR project is run by IOM, with local authorities and migrant-support associations; it takes departures until 2 February 2027 <sup>[256](#o256), [253](#o253)</sup>.

   You'll need: your passport or, without one, a Travel Permit from the Bangladesh mission, which the programme pays for ([Step 1: Get a passport or Travel Permit](#step-1-get-a-passport-or-travel-permit)) <sup>[255](#o255)</sup>.

   1. Contact IOM or a migrant-support association working on AVR; the F.A.S.T.E.R. project gives counselling to prepare departure and reintegration <sup>[253](#o253), [256](#o256)</sup>.
   2. A representative you give a mandate to can help with the application; since April 2026 the State pays that representative at the end of the procedure <sup>[257](#o257), [256](#o256)</sup>.
   3. Once you are admitted, the Prefettura tells the Questura, and any expulsion order is suspended <sup>[253](#o253)</sup>.
   4. The project gives guidance, pays the travel document and the ticket to your final destination, gives a cash grant before departure or on arrival, and gives help in Bangladesh to restart work, now payable in goods, by money transfer or in cash <sup>[255](#o255), [256](#o256)</sup>.

   **Who can't:** anyone who has used AVR before, or who was expelled as a criminal penalty or is wanted on an arrest warrant <sup>[258](#o258), [253](#o253)</sup>.

   **If you drop out:** you are removed by police escort at once <sup>[253](#o253)</sup>.

   > **Need to know**
   > - What are IOM Italy's AVR phone number and offices for Bangladeshis in Rome and Milan?
   > - How much is the AVR cash grant, and the reintegration grant, in 2026?
   > - How long does AVR take from application to flight for a Bangladeshi?
   > - Will a new AVR programme take departures after 2 February 2027?
   > - How many of the 1,063 AVR departures in 2026 to 4 November went to Bangladesh <sup>[46](#o46)</sup>?

2. <a id="step-2-choose-how-to-leave-item-2"></a>**Leave on your own.** Buy a ticket and leave; a person without papers identified at the border on the way out is not expelled by force, but can be given a re-entry ban of 1 to 3 years from the day of exit <sup>[252](#o252)</sup>.
3. <a id="step-2-choose-how-to-leave-item-3"></a>**If you already have an expulsion order, ask for time to leave.** Ask the Prefect for a voluntary departure period of 7 to 30 days, which can also be done through AVR <sup>[252](#o252)</sup>. The Questura must explain this option with leaflets in several languages; if you don't ask, you are removed by escort <sup>[252](#o252)</sup>. The Questore can ask you to show lawful funds of 1 to 3 months of the assegno sociale (Italy's basic social allowance) <sup>[252](#o252)</sup>. Missing the deadline without good reason means removal by escort <sup>[252](#o252)</sup>.

<a id="step-3-at-the-airport"></a>

### 15.3 Step 3: At the airport

Police check people leaving Italy at the external border <sup>[252](#o252)</sup>. If you left under a voluntary departure order, keep proof that you left on time: the ban can then be revoked on request <sup>[252](#o252)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What proof of exit do the Italian border police give (stamp, form), and how is a request to revoke the ban sent from Bangladesh?

<a id="if-you-are-detained"></a>

### 15.4 If you are detained

A detainee admitted to AVR stays in the CPR until the flight, within the maximum detention time (see [Detention in a CPR](#detention-in-a-cpr)) <sup>[253](#o253)</sup>. Having no valid passport counts as a risk of flight and leads to removal by escort <sup>[252](#o252)</sup>. The Consulate General in Milan visits Bangladeshis held in Italian jails, contacts their families and advises on legal help (welfare.milan@gmail.com) <sup>[168 (2022)](#o168)</sup>.

<a id="bans-after-you-leave"></a>

### 15.5 Bans after you leave

- **After deportation:** you cannot re-enter without the Interior Minister's special authorisation; the ban lasts 3 to 5 years <sup>[252](#o252), [5](#o5)</sup>.
- **Re-entering in breach of the ban:** 1 to 4 years in prison and a new expulsion by escort <sup>[252](#o252)</sup>.
- **After voluntary departure:** the ban runs from the end of the time given and can be revoked if you prove you left in time <sup>[252](#o252)</sup>.
- **After leaving on your own and being identified at the border:** 1 to 3 years <sup>[252](#o252)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does leaving through AVR bring an entry ban, and does it block a later Decreto Flussi visa?

<a id="sending-money-home"></a>

## 16 Sending money home

**Send through a licensed money-transfer company, Poste Italiane (the Italian post office) or a bank, never hundi** (money passed through informal brokers outside the banking system) <sup>[N34 (2024)](#n34), [259](#o259), [N86](#n86)</sup>. Hundi is illegal in Bangladesh, and in Italy running a banking or payment business without authorisation is a crime <sup>[N34 (2024)](#n34), [259](#o259)</sup>. By June 2026 Bangladesh's Financial Intelligence Unit (BFIU) had frozen or suspended about 55,000 mobile-money accounts linked to digital hundi and online gambling <sup>[N35](#n35)</sup>. Money sent legally earns the family the government's 2.5% cash incentive, kept in the FY2026-27 budget, and in some cases another 2.5% from the bank <sup>[260](#o260), [N87](#n87)</sup>. Italy charges no tax on money sent home: its 2018 tax of 1.5% on transfers above EUR 10 (about Tk 1,400) to non-EU countries was repealed by the 2021 Budget Law <sup>[N88 (2021)](#n88), [1](#o1)</sup>.

<a id="your-salary-and-bank-account"></a>

### 16.1 Your salary and bank account

Since 1 July 2018 every employer in Italy must pay wages, and any advance, through a bank or post office by a traceable method, never in cash; an employer who pays cash is fined EUR 1,000 to 5,000 (about Tk 1.4 lakh to Tk 7 lakh) <sup>[57 (2018)](#o57), [1](#o1)</sup>. Payment onto a prepaid card in your name counts, even a card without an IBAN (international bank account number) <sup>[57 (2018)](#o57)</sup>. Your signature on the payslip (busta paga) does not prove you were paid, so keep your bank statements as proof (see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid)) <sup>[57 (2018)](#o57)</sup>.

1. <a id="your-salary-and-bank-account-item-1"></a>**Open a conto di base (basic bank account).** Banks, Poste Italiane and other payment providers must offer it to everyone staying legally in the EU, not only in Italy, including people with no fixed address, refugees and asylum seekers; you need not be resident in Italy <sup>[261](#o261)</sup>. It gives transfers, salary credit, a bank card, cash withdrawals and deposits, and automatic bill payment <sup>[N89](#n89)</sup>. It is free if your ISEE (indicatore della situazione economica equivalente, the official family means figure) is below EUR 11,600 (about Tk 16.2 lakh) a year; above that it still costs much less than an ordinary account <sup>[N89](#n89), [261](#o261), [1](#o1)</sup>. You can't open one if you already have another current account in Italy, unless you close it, or use it for a business <sup>[N89](#n89)</sup>.

   You'll need:
   - your codice fiscale (Italian tax code), even a numeric provisional one <sup>[N51 (2022)](#n51)</sup>;
   - your permesso di soggiorno (residence permit), or the post-office receipt for your permit application or renewal with your photo on it; an Italian identity card is not needed <sup>[N51 (2022)](#n51)</sup>.

   1. Go to a bank branch or post office and say: "Vorrei aprire un conto di base. Il mio ISEE è sotto 11.600 euro, quindi deve essere gratuito." ("I want to open a basic account. My ISEE is below EUR 11,600, so it must be free.") <sup>[N89](#n89)</sup>
   2. Show the codice fiscale and the permit or receipt <sup>[N51 (2022)](#n51)</sup>.

   **If it fails:** in 2026 Banca d'Italia (Italy's central bank) found that banks resisted opening basic accounts for people legally in the EU but not resident, people seen as a money-laundering risk and people with little money, and had computer systems that shut out numeric provisional tax codes and permit receipts, while customers reported being offered dearer accounts instead <sup>[261](#o261)</sup>; only 180,000 basic accounts existed at the end of 2025 against 7 million people eligible <sup>[N89](#n89)</sup>. It told banks that your personal or money situation is no reason to refuse, that nobody may be refused just for belonging to a group seen as risky, that the basic account must always be offered next to any other account, that their systems must accept those documents, and that their compliance and internal audit staff must check by 30 November 2026 that every obstacle is gone <sup>[261](#o261)</sup>. A bank that refuses should tell you promptly why, unless the reason is public order, security or money laundering, and that you can turn to Banca d'Italia or the ABF (Arbitro Bancario Finanziario, the banking ombudsman) <sup>[261](#o261)</sup>. If a bank refuses your numeric codice fiscale, permit or receipt, go to a patronato (a free welfare advice office, most run by trade unions), a trade union or a migrants' association such as ASGI (Associazione per gli Studi Giuridici sull'Immigrazione, a migration lawyers' association) <sup>[N51 (2022)](#n51)</sup>. Then complain in writing (see [If the money doesn't arrive](#if-the-money-doesnt-arrive), which gives the same complaint route for banks) <sup>[262](#o262)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which banks and Poste Italiane branches in Rome and Milan open a conto di base for a Decreto Flussi (quota work visa) worker holding only his first permit receipt, and how many days does it take?
> - Where does a newly arrived worker get the ISEE certificate needed for a free basic account, and what does it cost?
> - Do Bangladeshi workers mostly take their salary on a Poste Italiane Postepay Evolution card or a bank account, and what does each cost a month?

<a id="how-to-send"></a>

### 16.2 How to send

You'll need:
- for a bKash wallet (a Bangladeshi mobile-money account): the receiver's bKash number and full name exactly as registered with bKash <sup>[N87](#n87)</sup>;

1. <a id="how-to-send-item-1"></a>**Check the company is licensed.** Banks and payment institutions are on Banca d'Italia's public registers at [infostat.bancaditalia.it/GIAVAInquiry-public/ng/](https://infostat.bancaditalia.it/GIAVAInquiry-public/ng/) <sup>[259](#o259)</sup>. In 2020 the Guardia di Finanza (Italy's financial police) seized a payment institution and six money-transfer shops in Rome run by Bangladeshis, accused of moving over EUR 20 million to Bangladesh without identifying the senders <sup>[N90 (2020)](#n90)</sup>. The case grew out of a 2019 investigation into a Bangladeshi network that, for profit, helped compatriots stay in Italy illegally <sup>[N90 (2020)](#n90)</sup>.
2. <a id="how-to-send-item-2"></a>**Choose the channel.** bKash lists these partners for sending from Italy straight to a bKash wallet: Ria, Remitly, National Exchange, Placid Express, WorldRemit, Wise, NEC Money, Taptap Send, Western Union, MoneyGram and ACE Money Transfer <sup>[N87](#n87)</sup>. In the World Bank's survey of August-September 2025, sending EUR 140 (about Tk 19,500) cost, fee and exchange-rate margin together <sup>[N86](#n86), [1](#o1)</sup>:
   - 2.98% (fee EUR 0.99, about Tk 140) online with a debit card through Poste Italiane's Western Union service, for cash pickup, the cheapest <sup>[N86](#n86), [1](#o1)</sup>;
   - 4.57% (fee EUR 2.50, about Tk 350) in cash at a National Exchange Company agent, to a bank account <sup>[N86](#n86), [1](#o1)</sup>;
   - 4.81% (fee EUR 3, about Tk 420) in cash at a Janata Exchange Company branch, to a bank account <sup>[N86](#n86), [1](#o1)</sup>;
   - 5.46% (fee EUR 5, about Tk 700) in cash at a post office through Poste Italiane's MoneyGram service <sup>[N86](#n86), [1](#o1)</sup>;
   - 22.24% at Extrabanca (fee EUR 25, about Tk 3,500) and 33.57% by bank transfer from Intesa Sanpaolo (fee EUR 47, about Tk 6,600) <sup>[N86](#n86), [1](#o1)</sup>.
3. <a id="how-to-send-item-3"></a>**Compare the rate, not only the fee.** The total cost is the fee plus the gap between the market rate and the rate the company gives you <sup>[N86](#n86)</sup>. On 10 November 2026 the market rate was about Tk 139.6 per euro <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.
4. <a id="how-to-send-item-4"></a>**Send.** At the counter, give the bKash number and registered name (or the bank details) and hand over the cash; or send from the company's app with your card <sup>[N87](#n87), [N86](#n86)</sup>. A bKash wallet takes up to Tk 2.5 lakh per transfer, any number of times <sup>[N87](#n87)</sup>.
5. <a id="how-to-send-item-5"></a>**The family collects.** bKash remittance can be cashed out for Tk 7 per thousand at BRAC Bank, City Bank and Q-Cash ATMs, and the 2.5% government incentive is added <sup>[N87](#n87)</sup>.

Undocumented workers have no formal channel for sending money <sup>[N34 (2024)](#n34)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What ID does a money-transfer shop in Italy ask for, and can a worker with only a renewal receipt, or an expired permit, still send money legally?
> - Which companies do Bangladeshis in Rome and Milan use most in 2026, and what taka rate per euro do they give?

<a id="if-the-money-doesnt-arrive"></a>

### 16.3 If the money doesn't arrive

Complain in this order:

1. <a id="if-the-money-doesnt-arrive-item-1"></a>**The company or bank,** by written complaint (reclamo) to its customer service; it normally has 60 days to answer, and 15 working days for payment services such as money transfers <sup>[262](#o262)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-the-money-doesnt-arrive-item-2"></a>**The ABF (Arbitro Bancario Finanziario, the banking ombudsman run with Banca d'Italia),** after your written complaint to the company, within 12 months of it; it costs EUR 20 (about Tk 2,800), repaid by the company if you win even in part <sup>[262](#o262), [1](#o1)</sup>. Information and past decisions: [arbitrobancariofinanziario.it](https://www.arbitrobancariofinanziario.it) <sup>[262](#o262)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-the-money-doesnt-arrive-item-3"></a>**bKash,** for a wallet transfer: +88-02-55663001 from abroad, 16247 in Bangladesh, support@bkash.com <sup>[N87](#n87)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Can a worker file an ABF complaint in English, and how long does the ABF take to decide?

<a id="tax-on-your-pay"></a>

### 16.4 Tax on your pay

IRPEF (imposta sul reddito delle persone fisiche, Italy's income tax) is 23% on income up to EUR 28,000 a year (about Tk 39 lakh) and 33% from EUR 28,000 to 50,000 (about Tk 70 lakh), after the 2026 Budget Law cut the middle rate from 35% <sup>[N91](#n91), [1](#o1)</sup>. Since 2025 a worker who is not an Italian, EU or EEA (European Economic Area) citizen gets no tax deduction for family members living abroad, such as a wife, children or parents in Bangladesh <sup>[263](#o263), [264](#o264)</sup>. For family living in Italy, the yearly deduction until 31 December 2026 is up to EUR 800 (about Tk 1.1 lakh) for a wife or husband, EUR 950 (about Tk 1.3 lakh) for each child aged 21 to 29, and EUR 750 (about Tk 1 lakh) for each parent living with you, each shrinking as your income rises <sup>[264](#o264), [263](#o263), [1](#o1)</sup>. A family member counts only if their own income is no more than EUR 2,840.51 a year (about Tk 4 lakh), or EUR 4,000 (about Tk 5.6 lakh) for a child up to 24 <sup>[264](#o264), [1](#o1)</sup>. The article of the income tax code that sets these family deductions (TUIR article 12) is repealed from 1 January 2027 <sup>[265](#o265)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What replaces the TUIR article 12 family deductions from 1 January 2027 under Legislative Decree 117/2026, and does any of it cover family in Bangladesh?
> - How much IRPEF and INPS (Istituto nazionale della previdenza sociale, the national social security institute) contribution is taken from a farm or construction worker's typical monthly pay?
> - How much does a CAF (centro di assistenza fiscale, tax-help centre) charge a worker to file the yearly 730 tax return?

<a id="if-a-worker-dies-is-injured-or-loses-the-job"></a>

## 17 If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job

In Italy, INAIL (Istituto nazionale per l'assicurazione contro gli infortuni sul lavoro, the national work-accident insurer) pays for work injuries, and INPS pays unemployment benefit <sup>[155](#o155), [65](#o65)</sup>. In Bangladesh the Wage Earners' Welfare Board (the Welfare Board, the government board for migrants' welfare) pays grants to members' families and runs the Probashi call centre, 16135 <sup>[179](#o179), [82](#o82)</sup>. The Bangladesh Embassy in Rome and Consulate General in Milan issue the papers to send a body home <sup>[266 (2021)](#o266), [267](#o267)</sup>.

<a id="if-you-are-injured"></a>

### 17.1 If you are injured

1. <a id="if-you-are-injured-item-1"></a>**Call 112 in an emergency,** Italy's emergency number <sup>[210](#o210)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-you-are-injured-item-2"></a>**Tell the employer at once,** even for a small injury; if you don't and the employer doesn't know, the days before the employer learns of it are not paid <sup>[154](#o154)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-you-are-injured-item-3"></a>**Know what INAIL pays while you can't work:** see Injury at work <sup>[155](#o155)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-you-are-injured-item-4"></a>**If the employer won't report the injury or you worked off the books,** a patronato serves workers working legally in Italy, and the national anti-trafficking helpline, 800 290 290, takes calls from victims of exploitation, anonymously, 24 hours a day <sup>[8](#o8), [191](#o191)</sup>.
5. <a id="if-you-are-injured-item-5"></a>**Going home injured.** Welfare Board membership covers bringing home workers in distress, and the Board's ambulance takes injured or sick workers free from Dhaka or Chattogram airport <sup>[179](#o179), [268](#o268)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Who issues the INAIL injury certificate (the hospital emergency department or the family doctor), and must the worker keep or send a copy?
> - What does INAIL pay for permanent disability, and can it pay into a Bangladeshi bank account after the worker goes home?
> - What can an injured worker do when an off-the-books employer refuses to report the accident?
> - Does the Tk 10 lakh migrant-worker insurance (see [If a worker dies, item 5: The insurance](#if-a-worker-dies-item-5)) pay for permanent disability, and how much for partial disability?

<a id="if-a-worker-dies"></a>

### 17.2 If a worker dies

These steps are for a relative or friend in Italy and the family in Bangladesh.

1. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-1"></a>**Get the death certificate** from the Italian hospital or the comune (town hall) <sup>[267](#o267)</sup>. To also register the death in Bangladesh, one relative fills in the form at [bdris.gov.bd/dr/application](https://bdris.gov.bd/dr/application) and brings the Italian death certificate to the Consulate in Milan <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-2"></a>**Ask the Embassy or Consulate for the nulla osta (no-objection letter) to send the body home.** It is free; the mission pays the air freight if the worker was a Welfare Board member, or on application if the family is poor <sup>[266 (2021)](#o266), [267](#o267)</sup>. Email Rome's labour welfare wing, welfare.rome@gmail.com, or in Milan fslbcgmilan@gmail.com and mission.milan@mofa.gov.bd <sup>[266 (2021)](#o266), [267](#o267)</sup>.

   You'll need <sup>[266 (2021)](#o266), [267](#o267)</sup>:
   - the death certificate;
   - the original passport and a copy;
   - the name, phone number and ID of a Bangladeshi in Italy who handles the transport;
   - the name, phone number and ID of the person receiving the body in Bangladesh;
   - a copy of the Welfare Board membership card, if any.

   Sample email: "My [relation], [name], passport [number], worked for [employer, phone] in [town], Italy, and died on [date] at [hospital or place]. We ask for the nulla osta to send the body to [district], Bangladesh. The person handling transport in Italy is [name, phone]; the receiver in Bangladesh is [name, phone]. Membership: [Welfare Board card number, or none]."

   > **Need to know**
   > - What does sending a body from Italy to Dhaka cost in 2026, funeral company and air freight included?
   > - Who pays when the worker was not a Welfare Board member and the family is not judged poor?
   > - How many days does the mission take to issue the nulla osta?
   > - How does a family arrange burial in Italy instead?

3. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-3"></a>**At Dhaka airport,** the Welfare Board's desk gives a Tk 35,000 cheque for transport and burial, and its ambulance carries the body free from Dhaka or Chattogram airport <sup>[179](#o179), [4](#o4), [268](#o268)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-4"></a>**The Tk 3 lakh grant.** The Welfare Board pays the family of a member who dies abroad a Tk 3 lakh grant <sup>[179](#o179)</sup>. Bangladeshis in Italy aged 18 to 65 can become members at the Embassy or Consulate (see [Help from the Bangladesh Embassy in Rome and Consulate in Milan](#help-from-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-rome-and-consulate-in-milan)) <sup>[269](#o269), [179](#o179), [232](#o232)</sup>.
5. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-5"></a>**The insurance.** Workers who left with BMET (Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) clearance since 12 March 2023 are insured for Tk 10 lakh for 5 years, bought with a one-time Tk 1,000 premium paid at clearance (["Work visa: Decreto Flussi worker (non-seasonal)"](#work-visa-decreto-flussi-worker-non-seasonal), [Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance and the flight](#step-6-orientation-bmet-clearance-and-the-flight)) <sup>[54](#o54), [4](#o4)</sup>. Call 16135 (+8809610102030 from abroad) to start a claim <sup>[82](#o82)</sup>.
6. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-item-6"></a>**Italian pension.** If the worker died after pensionable age, the family can get a survivors' pension under the general rules <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does BMET clearance for Italy make a worker a Welfare Board member for the Tk 3 lakh grant, or must he also join at the Embassy or Consulate?
> - Which papers does the family need for the Tk 35,000 airport cheque, the Tk 3 lakh grant and the insurance, and where does it apply?
> - What does INAIL pay the family in Bangladesh after a fatal work accident, and how does a widow in Bangladesh claim it?
> - Can heirs in Bangladesh claim the worker's unpaid wages and TFR (trattamento di fine rapporto, end-of-service pay), and through whom?

<a id="if-you-lose-the-job"></a>

### 17.3 If you lose the job

1. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-1"></a>**Your permit stays valid.** Losing the job, even by resigning, does not cancel your permit or your family's (see [Your residence permit](#your-residence-permit), If you lose your job) <sup>[8](#o8)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-2"></a>**If you were dismissed,** challenge it in writing within 60 days (see [If you aren't paid](#if-you-arent-paid)) <sup>[180](#o180)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-3"></a>**Claim NASpI (Nuova Assicurazione Sociale per l'Impiego, unemployment pay).**

   Who gets it: employees who lost the job against their will, with at least 13 weeks of contributions in the last four years <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>. Not people who resigned, unless for just cause (forced out by the employer's conduct); not holders of a seasonal permit; not fixed-term farm workers <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.

   How much and how long: 75% of average monthly pay up to a yearly reference amount, falling 3% a month from the sixth month, for half the weeks you contributed in the last four years <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.

   1. Apply online on inps.it within 68 days of the job ending, or lose the right; apply within 8 days to be paid from the eighth day <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.
   2. If you can't apply online, call the INPS contact centre, 803 164 (free from a landline) or 06 164 164 (from a mobile), or go to a patronato <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.
   3. Give a bank or postal account in your own name or joint name (see [Your salary and bank account](#your-salary-and-bank-account)) <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.

   A trip to Bangladesh for reasons other than looking for work keeps NASpI only while you keep its conditions <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-you-lose-the-job-item-4"></a>**Check your final pay,** including the TFR (see [Changing employer or leaving](#changing-employer-or-leaving), Leaving at the end of the job) <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What login (SPID, Italy's digital identity, or CIE, the electronic ID card) does a Bangladeshi worker need to claim NASpI on inps.it, and do patronati charge to file it?
> - What must a NASpI claimant do to keep the payment during a trip to Bangladesh, and for how long?
> - Does the BMET insurance pay anything to a worker sent home after losing the job?

<a id="coming-home"></a>

## 18 Coming home

- **Leaving Italy with cash.** Declare EUR 10,000 (about Tk 14 lakh) or more in cash, or the same in other currencies, to Italian customs on the EU cash declaration form when you leave the EU <sup>[270](#o270), [271 (2024)](#o271), [1](#o1)</sup>. Gold bars of at least 99.5% gold and coins of at least 90% gold count as cash <sup>[270](#o270)</sup>. Customs can act below EUR 10,000 if they suspect the money is linked to crime, and not declaring is penalised <sup>[270](#o270)</sup>.
- **Customs in Dhaka.**
  - Up to 2 used mobile phones and 1 new phone once a year, without duty or tax <sup>[272](#o272)</sup>.
  - At most 117 g (10 tola) of gold, one bar, once a year, declared at customs <sup>[272](#o272)</sup>.
  - Declare foreign currency over USD 10,000 on the FMJ form <sup>[272](#o272)</sup>.
- **Airport help.** The Welfare Board's desks at Dhaka, Chattogram and Sylhet help with customs clearance and lost luggage, and take complaints from workers coming home after abuse or fraud <sup>[273](#o273), [4](#o4)</sup>. Dhaka desk: +8802 8901040, 01404 413388; Chattogram: 01913631797; Sylhet: 01558277904 <sup>[268](#o268), [4](#o4)</sup>. Women returning within a year after abuse or fraud get Tk 5,000, and sick or injured workers get a free ambulance from Dhaka or Chattogram airport <sup>[4](#o4), [268](#o268)</sup>.
- **A place to stay.** The Wage Earners' Centre near Shahjalal airport costs Tk 100 to enter and Tk 200 a night; book at wec.wewb.gov.bd <sup>[274](#o274)</sup>. The Probashi Lounge at Shahjalal airport gives free wifi and telephone and cheaper food to workers who show BMET clearance or Welfare Board membership <sup>[275](#o275)</sup>.
- **If your family needs legal help in Bangladesh,** apply to the Welfare Board directly, or through the expatriate welfare branch of the Deputy Commissioner's office (the district administration) or the DEMO (District Employment and Manpower Office, BMET's district office) <sup>[276](#o276)</sup>.
- **A business loan.** Probashi Kallyan Bank (PKB, the government bank for migrants) lends returnees up to Tk 50 lakh, up to Tk 3 lakh without collateral; you need an up-to-date trade licence <sup>[277](#o277)</sup>. Helpline 16238, or +880 9677-787878 from abroad <sup>[56](#o56)</sup>.
- **BRAC's help.** At Dhaka airport BRAC's Migration Programme has given returnees from Italy emergency help, counselling and money to get home, followed by counselling, training and financial help <sup>[N83](#n83)</sup>.
- **Children.** Welfare Board membership gives members' children scholarships and a migrant quota for school admission <sup>[179](#o179)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How does a returnee contact BRAC's Migration Programme for help after coming home?

<a id="pension-after-you-go-home"></a>

### 18.1 Pension after you go home

A worker on a non-seasonal contract who goes home for good keeps the pension rights earned in Italy, even if Italy has no social security agreement with the home country <sup>[157](#o157), [8](#o8)</sup>. Seasonal workers are excluded <sup>[157](#o157), [8](#o8)</sup>.

- **Hired after 1 January 1996:** you get a pension at Italy's pensionable age, given as 67 for 2023-2024 and rising with life expectancy, even with fewer than 20 years of contributions <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.
- **Hired before 1996:** you need 20 years of contributions <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.

At pensionable age, apply in one of these ways <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>:
- online, if you have INPS login details, attaching documents authenticated by the Italian consulate, then posting them to INPS, Direzione provinciale di Perugia, via Canali 5, 06122 Perugia;
- at the Italian consulate where you live, if you have no login;
- through a patronato.

> **Need to know**
> - Is there any social security agreement between Italy and Bangladesh?
> - Can a worker get his INPS contributions refunded when he leaves Italy for good?
> - How does a returnee have documents "authenticated by the Italian consulate" at the Italian Embassy in Dhaka, and what does it charge?
> - Does INPS pay pensions into a Bangladeshi bank account, and in which currency?

<a id="contacts"></a>

## 19 Contacts

<a id="help-from-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-rome-and-consulate-in-milan"></a>

### 19.1 Help from the Bangladesh Embassy in Rome and Consulate in Milan

Bangladesh has an Embassy in Rome, a Consulate General in Milan, and honorary consuls in Catania, Florence, Palermo, Genoa, Cagliari, Naples, Venice (Marghera) and Bari <sup>[120](#o120), [278 (2023)](#o278), [279](#o279)</sup>. The Milan Consulate also runs mobile consular services in other cities, such as Ravenna in July 2026 and Udine in September 2026 <sup>[280](#o280)</sup>. Amounts below are converted at about Tk 139.6 per euro <sup>[1](#o1)</sup>.

**Passport.**

1. <a id="help-from-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-rome-and-consulate-in-milan-item-1"></a>**Apply online** at [epassport.gov.bd/onboarding](https://www.epassport.gov.bd/onboarding), choosing country Italy and your mission; choosing a Bangladesh district sends the file to that district's passport office instead <sup>[281](#o281)</sup>. Machine-readable passports (MRP) stopped on 31 December 2025, so every application is for an e-passport <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>. Living abroad, you can apply with an English birth registration that is online instead of an NID (national ID card), unless your earlier MRP was made with an NID <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>. Fill in the form in Acrobat Reader, save it and reopen it offline to print, so the full small barcode shows: Milan cannot enrol a form with an incomplete, large barcode <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>.
2. <a id="help-from-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-rome-and-consulate-in-milan-item-2"></a>**Book an appointment:** Rome at [bdembassyrome.com](https://www.bdembassyrome.com); Milan at [passport.bcgmilan.it](https://passport.bcgmilan.it/), which opens every Thursday, asks for the OID number from the online form and confirms by email within 72 hours <sup>[234](#o234), [281](#o281)</sup>. Milan also gives dates for urgent cases through a [Google form](https://docs.google.com/forms/d/1QZ_aqFhPqt6wrLZXHddZrQerC25w_AA1VdCaY0aIZfo/viewform), and a newborn's first passport needs no appointment there <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>. At enrolment, everyone aged 12 or over gives fingerprints and an iris scan, children aged 6 to 11 fingerprints only, and children under 6 neither <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>.
3. <a id="help-from-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-rome-and-consulate-in-milan-item-3"></a>**Pay by bank card;** the Rome Embassy takes no cash <sup>[234](#o234)</sup>. Rome charges EUR 130 (about Tk 18,100) for 10 years and EUR 105 (about Tk 14,700) for 5 years <sup>[232](#o232), [1](#o1)</sup>. In Milan, students get a discount with a student certificate (ছাত্রত্ব সনদ) from their school, university or comune (municipality) <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>.
4. <a id="help-from-the-bangladesh-embassy-in-rome-and-consulate-in-milan-item-4"></a>**Wait and collect.** Passports are printed in Dhaka; at the Rome Embassy they usually arrive within 2 months of enrolment, and there is no emergency passport service <sup>[234](#o234)</sup>. Check progress with the ITA100000... number on your enrolment slip; return by DHL usually costs EUR 10 to 20 (about Tk 1,400 to Tk 2,800) <sup>[234](#o234), [1](#o1)</sup>. To collect in Milan, bring the customer copy of the receipt, the enrolment slip or a copy of the old passport, the previous original passport, and the police report if it was lost; a parent comes for a minor <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>.

You can renew when 6 months or less are left <sup>[234](#o234)</sup>. If your passport is lost in Italy, file a denuncia (police report) at the Questura (provincial police headquarters) or the Carabinieri (the military police force), and bring the original report with a copy of the old passport and your NID or English birth certificate <sup>[281](#o281), [282](#o282)</sup>. Without an NID, a birth certificate and a valid permesso di soggiorno (residence permit) let you correct your name, parents' names or age (by up to 8 years), but Milan took these applications only until 30 June 2026 <sup>[282](#o282), [233](#o233)</sup>. For a Travel Permit to go home without a valid passport, see [Going home without valid papers](#going-home-without-valid-papers), [Step 1: Get a passport or Travel Permit](#step-1-get-a-passport-or-travel-permit) <sup>[232](#o232)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - The Rome Embassy's FAQ gives EUR 100 for any passport <sup>[234](#o234)</sup>; which fee does it charge at the counter in November 2026, and what do the Milan Consulate's passport fee and student discount come to?

**NID.** Apply at the Rome Embassy with the online form, your English birth registration and its online-verified copy, your valid passport and a copy, and one photo <sup>[283 (2024)](#o283)</sup>. To correct details in a passport, an adult needs the NID and an undertaking (অঙ্গীকারনামা, a signed declaration), and a minor under 18 the birth registration and an undertaking <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - After 30 June 2026, can a Bangladeshi in Italy without an NID still correct a passport in Rome or Milan with a birth registration and a permesso di soggiorno?

**Power of attorney** (to let someone in Bangladesh act for you, for example on land or a court case). Bring a written power of attorney with the full names, dates of birth and document numbers of everyone giving and receiving it, 2 passport photos of each, copies of the givers' valid passports and the receivers' passports or NIDs; everyone giving it must come in person <sup>[284](#o284)</sup>. Rome charges EUR 50 (about Tk 7,000) for the first 5 pages, then EUR 5 (about Tk 700) a page <sup>[232](#o232), [1](#o1)</sup>.

**Certificates.** The Milan Consulate issues family, dependent, unmarried, marriage, divorce, nationality, education, income and other certificates against your valid passport and a copy, and you come in person <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>. The Bangladeshi marriage and divorce deeds, family details certificate, parent's affidavit and unmarried certificate must first be attested by the Law Ministry and MoFA (Bangladesh's Ministry of Foreign Affairs) in Dhaka and apostilled, and school certificates by the Education Ministry and MoFA (see ["Work visa: Decreto Flussi worker (non-seasonal)"](#work-visa-decreto-flussi-worker-non-seasonal), [Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates](#step-4-passport-police-clearance-and-certificates)) <sup>[233](#o233), [86](#o86)</sup>. Also bring:
- **Family certificate (paribarik sanad):** the nikahnama (marriage deed) and every family member's birth registration or passport copy <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>.
- **Dependent certificate:** the family details certificate from the Pourashava (municipality) or City Corporation, or every member's birth registration, passport or NID; for a student, a parent's affidavit on non-judicial stamp paper giving the family's yearly income, or the same family documents <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>.
- **Unmarried certificate:** one from your home district's Deputy Commissioner (the district administrator), less than 6 months old, handed in 7 working days early, because MoFA in Dhaka verifies it <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>.
- **Marriage or divorce certificate:** your spouse's passport or NID copy and the nikahnama or talaknama (divorce deed); for a marriage by phone the nikahnama must say so, and you add copies of both sides' witnesses' passport, carta d'identità (Italian ID card) and permesso di soggiorno, and the Italian address and phone number used <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>.
- **Nationality certificate:** a recent passport-size colour photo <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>.
- **Birth certificate in Italian:** a copy of your birth registration <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>.
- **Same person certificate** (for an Italian document with your name or place wrong): a copy of that document <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>.
- **Income certificate:** your original Italian income statement (CUD, Redditi, Unico or Telematico) <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>.
- **Lost passport certificate:** every previous passport and the original denuncia <sup>[233](#o233)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does the Milan Consulate charge for each certificate and for a power of attorney, and how many working days does each take?

**Welfare Board membership.** Workers aged 18 to 65 who came to Italy on their own can join at the mission <sup>[269](#o269)</sup>. Fill in the form at [portal.wewb.gov.bd/register](https://portal.wewb.gov.bd/register), then hand in the printout with copies of your passport and permesso di soggiorno, a colour photo, a return envelope (DHL or TNT) and EUR 32 (about Tk 4,500); Milan needs no appointment and takes applications every day until 1 pm <sup>[269](#o269), [179](#o179), [232](#o232), [1](#o1)</sup>. What membership pays is under If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job and Coming home <sup>[179](#o179)</sup>.

**If a Bangladeshi dies in Italy,** the mission issues the nulla osta to send the body home (see [If a worker dies](#if-a-worker-dies)) <sup>[266 (2021)](#o266), [267](#o267)</sup>.

**Legal help.** If you face a court case and can't pay a lawyer, the Milan Consulate issues a no-income certificate (a certificate of financial hardship) so you can get gratuito patrocinio, a free lawyer paid by the Italian state <sup>[169 (2022)](#o169), [233](#o233)</sup>. Bring your original passport and a copy, the case papers from the police, Carabinieri or Guardia di Finanza (Italy's financial police), and a parent's affidavit on non-judicial stamp paper giving the family's yearly income, attested by the Law Ministry and MoFA and apostilled, or every family member's birth registration, passport or NID copy, and come for an interview in person <sup>[285 (2022)](#o285), [233](#o233)</sup>. The Consulate also visits Bangladeshis in Italian jails and contacts their families (welfare.milan@gmail.com) <sup>[168 (2022)](#o168)</sup>. The Rome Embassy's labour welfare wing visits workplaces to check conditions and timely pay (welfare.rome@gmail.com) <sup>[178 (2021)](#o178)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does the Rome Embassy's labour welfare wing take unpaid-wage complaints against Italian employers, and what does it do with them?
